`playbooks/scenarios/civil-unrest.md`:

```markdown
---
id: civil-unrest
title: Civil unrest and breakdown of order
icon: fire
order: 9
summary: Rioting, looting and arson, police that cannot answer, curfews and emergency regulations. Staying in, staying lawful and keeping the street together.
modules: [security-law, community, evacuation, comms, medical, mental-health, food]
overlays: [health, rail, fuel]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: 2011 England riots (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2011_England_riots
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: 2024 United Kingdom riots (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2024_United_Kingdom_riots
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Public Order Act 1986
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1986/64/contents
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Civil Contingencies Act 2004
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2004/36/contents
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Criminal Justice and Immigration Act 2008 section 76
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2008/4/section/76
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Military aid to the civil power (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Military_aid_to_the_civil_power
    as_at: 2026-02-15
---

## Right now

**Go in, stay in, tune in.** The register's disorder scenario is criminal damage, arson, rioting and looting at several flashpoints in urban areas at once, with injuries to the public and responders and health services under pressure ([NRR 2025, p. 173](doc:nrr-2025#page=173)). Nobody was killed by going home early. Lock doors, close curtains, move the car off the street if you can do it quietly, and keep away from windows on the street side.

**Do not go to watch, and do not go to help unless asked.** The register's own words: turning up at the scene "could be dangerous and overwhelm the emergency services" ([NRR 2025, p. 23](doc:nrr-2025#page=23)). Five people died in the 2011 riots, three of them protecting shops ([2011 England riots](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2011_England_riots)).

**Fire is the killer in a riot.** Test the smoke alarms, keep the stairs clear, know the way out of the back, and keep buckets of water where an arson attack on the front door would need them ([Shelter and heat module](module:shelter-heat)). Call 999 for fire; fires in riots get answered even when burglaries do not.

**Rumours.** Every riot runs on messages that are half true; check with the SHARE checklist before believing or forwarding ([NRR 2025, p. 22](doc:nrr-2025#page=22)). Emergency Alerts and BBC local radio are the official sources ([How alerts work](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/alerts/how-alerts-work)).

{{module:security-law}}

## First 72 hours

- **Curfews and dispersal orders** are lawful under the Public Order Act 1986 and its successors; obey them ([Public Order Act 1986](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1986/64/contents); [Public Order Act 2023](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2023/15/contents)). Under the Civil Contingencies Act 2004 ministers can restrict movement and assembly by regulation ([Civil Contingencies Act 2004](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2004/36/contents)).
- **Force.** You may use reasonable force to protect yourself, others and property; a householder facing an intruder is judged only on whether the force was grossly disproportionate ([CJIA 2008 s76](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2008/4/section/76)). Carrying anything as a weapon in the street, or lying in wait, is an offence, and the courts after 2011 gave riot-related offences heavier sentences than normal ([2011 England riots](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2011_England_riots)).
- **Looting is theft and burglary** whatever the crowd is doing ([Theft Act 1968 s1](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1968/60/section/1)); over 1,000 people were charged within weeks of the 2024 riots ([2024 United Kingdom riots](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2024_United_Kingdom_riots)).
- **Supplies out of sight.** Nothing visible from the street: no generator noise, no full shelves in the window, bins put out late ([Security and the law module](module:security-law)).
- **The street.** Neighbours who know each other, share numbers and a PMR446 channel and check on the vulnerable are the only security that scales ([Community module](module:community)); patrols and "defence groups" are how residents end up arrested or hurt.
- **Medical:** A&E will be full; treat cuts, burns and smoke inhalation at home with the cards, and call 999 only for life-threatening cases ([Burns card](card:burns); [Severe bleeding card](card:severe-bleeding)).

{{module:community}}

{{module:comms}}

{{module:medical}}

## First month

- **Strikes and shortages.** Disorder and industrial action travel together; the register plans for prolonged strikes in critical roles with services "at a reduced capacity", and the Civil Contingencies Act obliges key organisations to plan for mass absence ([NRR 2025, p. 175](doc:nrr-2025#page=175)). Shops close early, deliveries stop in affected areas, cash machines are looted or empty; two weeks of food and cash carry you past the worst ([Food module](module:food)).
- **Movement.** Stations and fuel stations near trouble close; plan journeys away from the map's marked stations and forecourts and travel in daylight ([stations and fuel overlay](map:?overlay=rail&overlay=fuel)).
- **Evacuation** is rarely needed, but a house that has been attacked or is next to a fire is left with the grab bag by the back way, to family or the council's rest centre ([Evacuation module](module:evacuation)).
- **Insurance and compensation:** photograph damage, report it to the police for a crime number, and claim; keep politics out of every conversation with neighbours, insurers and the police ([Community module](module:community)).
- **Children:** keep them in, keep routines, answer honestly; distress that lasts weeks needs help ([Coping with trauma](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/coping-with-trauma/)).

{{module:food}}

{{module:evacuation}}

## Long term

Order in Britain has broken locally and briefly, never nationally for long: the 1926 general strike lasted nine days ([1926 United Kingdom general strike](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/1926_United_Kingdom_general_strike)), the 2011 riots five ([2011 England riots](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2011_England_riots)). If policing fails for months, the armed forces act under police control as military aid to the civil power, not as a replacement for law ([Military aid to the civil power](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Military_aid_to_the_civil_power)). Disorder that comes with war is the [invasion playbook](playbook:invasion). A street that has organised itself for a fortnight has the beginnings of the community plan in the [long rebuild playbook](playbook:long-rebuild). The Prepare advice on trauma applies to everyone who saw the worst of it ([Coping with trauma](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/coping-with-trauma/)).

{{module:mental-health}}

## UK specifics

- **Numbers:** 999 for danger to life and fire; 101 for everything else, which will not answer; Samaritans 116 123 ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Offences you can commit by accident:** riot, violent disorder and affray under the Public Order Act 1986 apply to anyone who joins in, including to "have a look" ([Public Order Act 1986](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1986/64/contents)); aggravated trespass and obstruction of the highway are the protest offences ([CJPOA 1994 s68](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1994/33/section/68)); assaulting a police officer is a separate offence under the Police Act 1996 ([Police Act 1996](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1996/16/contents)).
- **Weapons:** carrying a knife, a bat or a pepper spray "for protection" is an offence; the penknife rule is 3 inches, non-locking ([CJA 1988 s139](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1988/33/section/139); [Knife and firearms law](page:knife-firearms-law)).
- **Prevention of Crime Act 1953** makes carrying an offensive weapon in public an offence in its own right ([Prevention of Crime Act 1953](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Eliz2/1-2/14)).
- **Who runs the response:** the police lead; Local Resilience Forums coordinate councils, fire and health; councils open rest centres ([LRF contacts](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/local-resilience-forums-contact-details)).
- **Scotland and Northern Ireland** have their own public order law and, in Northern Ireland, their own history; the numbers and the go-in-stay-in rule are the same ([Security and the law module](module:security-law)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Everyone home; doors locked, curtains closed, car moved quietly off the street {#everyone-home}
- [ ] Smoke alarms tested; stairs clear; back way out known; buckets of water by the front door {#fire-ready}
- [ ] Radio on BBC local; Emergency Alerts on; SHARE checklist before forwarding anything {#official-sources}
- [ ] Nothing valuable or edible visible from the street {#nothing-visible}
- [ ] Street numbers list and PMR446 channel agreed; vulnerable neighbours named {#street-network}
- [ ] First-aid kit out: dressings, burns kit, gloves {#first-aid-out}
- [ ] Two weeks of food and cash in the house {#two-weeks-food-cash}
- [ ] Grab bag by the back door in case of fire or attack {#grab-bag-back-door}
- [ ] Curfew times and any emergency regulations written down and obeyed {#know-the-orders}
- [ ] Damage photographed and reported for a crime number {#photograph-report}

## Go deeper
```

`playbooks/scenarios/economic-collapse.md`:

```markdown
---
id: economic-collapse
title: Economic collapse
icon: coins
order: 10
summary: Banks closed, cards dead, the pound failing, prices doubling monthly. Cash, the deposit guarantee, barter, and feeding the household without money.
modules: [food, community, security-law, growing-food, livestock, mental-health, vehicles-fuel]
overlays: [fuel, health]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Northern Rock (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Northern_Rock
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Financial Services Compensation Scheme (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Financial_Services_Compensation_Scheme
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Hyperinflation (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hyperinflation
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Corralito (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Corralito
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Rationing in the United Kingdom (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Local exchange trading system (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Local_exchange_trading_system
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Prepare, get prepared for emergencies
    kiwix: prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/
    as_at: 2026-09
---

## Right now

**If your bank's systems are down, wait; if the bank is failing, know the guarantee.** A systemically important bank's technology failure is planned for as customers unable to see balances, pay or draw cash from machines, with a partial outage for days after ([NRR 2025, p. 102](doc:nrr-2025#page=102)). A run, like Northern Rock in September 2007, is a queue that makes the failure it fears ([Northern Rock](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Northern_Rock)). Deposits are protected by the Financial Services Compensation Scheme up to its published limit per person per banking group; check the limit in the library rather than a rumour ([Financial Services Compensation Scheme](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Financial_Services_Compensation_Scheme)).

**Cash today.** Draw what you are allowed while machines work, in small notes; Argentina's 2001 "corralito" capped withdrawals overnight ([Corralito](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Corralito)). Buy what you will need, not what you can carry: the two-week food store, medicines, fuel, the things that break ([Prepare](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/)).

**Do not** sell at panic prices, take a loan you cannot service, or move savings on the strength of a message ([NRR 2025, p. 22](doc:nrr-2025#page=22)).

{{module:food}}

## First 72 hours

- **Payments.** Card terminals, Faster Payments and direct debits stop with the systems behind them; the register plans for a financial market infrastructure to be down for a week and partly down for weeks ([NRR 2025, p. 58](doc:nrr-2025#page=58)). A deliberate attack on those systems is the [cyber attack playbook](playbook:cyber-attack). Cash, cheques and IOUs between people who know each other keep a village trading ([Cheque](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cheque)).
- **Prices.** In hyperinflation money loses value between morning and evening; wages are paid daily and spent at once, and anyone holding notes loses ([Hyperinflation](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hyperinflation)). Hold goods and skills, not paper: food, fuel, tools, seed, and the ability to fix things.
- **Rent, mortgage, bills.** Talk to the lender and the landlord before missing a payment; keep written records of every conversation; the Universal Credit helpline and the council are the safety net that still exists ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Fuel.** Half a tank at all times and the lawful 30 litres stored outside the house ([Petroleum regulations 2014](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/1637/contents)); a bicycle costs nothing to run ([Vehicles and fuel module](module:vehicles-fuel)).
- **Neighbours.** The people who will trade with you in a month are the ones you help this week ([Community module](module:community)).

{{module:vehicles-fuel}}

{{module:community}}

## First month

- **Barter and local exchange.** Swapping goods and labour is lawful and always has been ([Barter](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Barter)); a local exchange trading system is a ledger of hours and favours that a village hall can run on paper ([Local exchange trading system](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Local_exchange_trading_system); [Time-based currency](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Time-based_currency)). Britain's local currencies, such as the Bristol pound, showed what works and what does not ([Local currency](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Local_currency)).
- **Rationing.** If the state rations, the wartime system is the model: registration with one shop, points, and a ration that kept people healthier than before the war ([Rationing in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom)). Hoarding beyond your household's need is what makes rationing necessary.
- **Grow and keep.** Potatoes at 2 to 3 kg a square metre, hens for eggs, rabbits for meat ([Growing food module](module:growing-food); [Livestock module](module:livestock)); the 1940 allotment campaign fed a nation that could not import ([Victory garden](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Victory_garden)).
- **Theft rises with prices.** Locks, lights, a dog, nothing visible, and a street that watches; no weapons, no traps ([Security and the law module](module:security-law)).
- **Mental health.** Debt, shame and unemployment drive suicide; ask directly, remove means, and use the lines that still answer ([Help for suicidal thoughts (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/mental-health/feelings-symptoms-behaviours/behaviours/help-for-suicidal-thoughts/); [Mental health module](module:mental-health)).

{{module:growing-food}}

{{module:livestock}}

{{module:security-law}}

## Long term

Currency failures end with a new currency, a peg or a foreign one: Germany's 1923 hyperinflation ended within months of the Rentenmark ([Hyperinflation in the Weimar Republic](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hyperinflation_in_the_Weimar_Republic)); Zimbabwe abandoned its dollar in 2009 ([Hyperinflation in Zimbabwe](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hyperinflation_in_Zimbabwe)). The lasting damage is to savings, pensions and trust. Britain's own depression of the 1930s and the 1970s Winter of Discontent were long, not fatal ([Great Depression in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Great_Depression_in_the_United_Kingdom); [Winter of Discontent](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Winter_of_Discontent)). The household that comes through has skills people pay for in any currency, a garden, a store, and no debt in a currency it cannot earn.

{{module:mental-health}}

## UK specifics

- **The guarantee.** The Financial Services Compensation Scheme pays within days when a bank fails, up to its limit per person per banking group; joint accounts count double; it is funded by the industry, not by whether the bank had money ([Financial Services Compensation Scheme](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Financial_Services_Compensation_Scheme)). Spread savings across banking groups, not brands of one group.
- **Who acts.** The Bank of England, the Treasury and the Financial Conduct Authority coordinate under the Authorities' Response Framework ([NRR 2025, p. 58](doc:nrr-2025#page=58)); the Bank is lender of last resort and issuer of notes ([Bank of England](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Bank_of_England)).
- **Energy prices** are the usual British trigger: the register's gas and oil scenarios are about price, with households needing support and vulnerable groups hit hardest ([NRR 2025, p. 62](doc:nrr-2025#page=62); [NRR 2025, p. 63](doc:nrr-2025#page=63)); the Energy Act 1976 gives ministers powers over fuel supply in a real shortage ([NRR 2025, p. 63](doc:nrr-2025#page=63)).
- **Cash.** Notes remain legal; a shop may refuse them and a bank may limit withdrawals, and neither is a reason to fight ([Cash](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cash)). Credit unions and the Post Office are the fallback for people a bank has dropped ([Credit union](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Credit_union)).
- **Benefits and bills:** the Universal Credit helpline, Cold Weather Payments in winter, and the Priority Services Register for anyone who cannot afford to lose power or water ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers); [Cold Weather Payment](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/cold-weather-payment)).
- **Taking from shops or fields is theft** in a crisis as in any other week ([Theft Act 1968 s1](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1968/60/section/1)); foraging for your own use is not ([Foraging law](page:foraging-law)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Cash for a month in small notes, kept in two places {#cash-month}
- [ ] Savings spread across banking groups; the FSCS limit checked {#spread-savings}
- [ ] Two weeks of food, medicines and the things that break bought while cards work {#buy-essentials-now}
- [ ] Lender, landlord and utilities spoken to before any missed payment; notes kept {#talk-to-creditors}
- [ ] Tank half full; bicycle serviced {#fuel-and-bike}
- [ ] Skills and goods to trade listed; the street's ledger started {#trade-ledger}
- [ ] Seed potatoes, seed and a bed dug; hens or rabbits considered {#grow-and-keep}
- [ ] Nothing valuable visible; locks and lights checked {#nothing-visible}
- [ ] One conversation a week with anyone in the household who has gone quiet {#check-in-weekly}
- [ ] Paper copies of bank statements, policies and payslips {#paper-records}
```

**Fact-check checklist (reviewer, Task 6):** open every citation; correct the text, never the citation, when they disagree.
- [ ] nuclear-war: the 7:10 numbers (7 h a tenth, 49 h a hundredth, 2 weeks a thousandth) against FEMA page 35; "12-24 hours" and "cars are not adequate shelters" on FEMA page 74; "first forty-eight hours" on Protect and Survive page 15; halving thicknesses on NWSS page 19; NRPB page 9 dose table (100 mg iodine, 169 mg iodate, 130 mg iodide for adults) and page 16 (over-40s); the KI-not-useful line on FEMA page 97; Faslane, Coulport, Aldermaston, Burghfield, Barrow, Devonport, Rosyth in the UK nuclear weapons article; "south-westerly" in the UK climate article; 1992 stand-down in the four-minute warning article.
- [ ] nuclear-accident: NRR pages 96, 98, 100 and 40 say what is quoted; REPPIR 2019 defines the Detailed Emergency Planning Zone and pre-distribution; the Windscale milk figure (500 km² for about a month) in the article; Welsh and Cumbrian sheep restrictions lifted in 2012 in the Chernobyl article; the French station names on the Channel coast.
- [ ] pandemic: 4%, 2.5% and nine months on NRR page 156; 2,000 cases, 25%, 200,000 contacts on page 158; the paracetamol dose on the NHS medicines page; the 92% saturation threshold in the pulse oximetry article; amoxicillin and doxycycline on WHO EML pages 12 and 18; 228,000 UK deaths in the Spanish flu article; the COVID-19 UK death count in its article; 5 and 8 days for registering a death.
- [ ] grid-collapse: "instantaneously and without warning" and the winter assumption on NRR page 90; hours, "skeletal network", "up to 7 days", "several months" on page 45; "1-4 days" and "more than 5 days" on page 139; Arwen and Eunice on page 92; 3-hour rolling cuts on page 94; the five preparedness steps on page 21; the FSA 4 h, 24 h, 48 h, 8 °C figures; 6 metres and the transfer switch on AD P page 7; 30 litres in the 2014 regulations; the Iberian blackout date and duration; the Storm Arwen and Storm Éowyn outage durations in their articles.
- [ ] solar-storm: "1-2 weeks", "several times during a 2-week period" and the list of effects on NRR page 137; "within a few hours" on page 86; the CME travel time and the 8-minute flare figure in their articles; the 1989 Quebec figures (90 seconds, nine hours, six million) in the March 1989 article; May 2024 aurora over the UK in its article.
- [ ] emp: E1, E2, E3 and the vehicle test findings in the nuclear EMP article; 1,400 km streetlights in the Starfish Prime article; the classified-scenario footnote on NRR page 184.
- [ ] cyber-attack: "up to 72 hours", "weeks or months", "within a fortnight" and the 999/112 line on NRR page 55; 2 days plus 2 days on page 59; a week plus weeks and "months" on page 58; Synnovis 10,152 and 1,710 on page 52; "even after boiling" on page 120; approximately 3 months and 3-hour cuts on page 43; WannaCry's 19,000 appointments in its article; CrowdStrike date 19 July 2024; Ukraine 2015 (230,000 customers, up to six hours); Colonial Pipeline May 2021.
- [ ] invasion: "conventional missiles and cyber operations" and civilian fatalities on NRR page 184; "several years" and gas on page 183; three to four weeks on page 177; the Channel Islands winter of 1944 and the Vega in the occupation article; 43,000 Blitz deaths; the civilian protections named from the Fourth Geneva Convention article; no tactical content anywhere in the file.
- [ ] civil-unrest: rioting, looting, arson and "flash points" on NRR page 173; strike planning and the CCA duty on page 175; five deaths in the 2011 article; the 2024 charging numbers in its article; the "grossly disproportionate" wording in s76; nine days for the 1926 strike.
- [ ] economic-collapse: page 102, 58, 62 and 63 wording; Northern Rock September 2007; the current FSCS limit and the per-banking-group rule from the FSCS article on the review date (the text deliberately gives no figure); the Rentenmark and 2009 Zimbabwe dates; potatoes 2 to 3 kg a square metre matches the growing-food module.

`playbooks/scenarios/supply-chain.md`:

```markdown

  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025

    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2021_United_Kingdom_fuel_supply_crisis

    as_at: 2026-09

    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/1637/contents
    as_at: 2026-09

## Right now

**Do not join the queue.** The September 2021 fuel crisis was a shortage of lorry drivers turned into empty forecourts by panic buying within two days ([2021 United Kingdom fuel supply crisis](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2021_United_Kingdom_fuel_supply_crisis)); the September 2000 refinery blockades had supermarkets warning of empty shelves within days ([Fuel protests in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fuel_protests_in_the_United_Kingdom)). If your tank is above half and your cupboard holds two weeks, you have already done the useful thing ([Prepare](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/)).

**Take stock tonight:** food by days, medicines by days, fuel by miles, cash by weeks, nappies, formula, pet food, gas bottles, wood. Write it on the fridge and buy only the gaps, in ordinary quantities, from ordinary shops ([Food module](module:food)).

**Medicines first.** Order repeat prescriptions early, ask the pharmacist about alternatives now rather than when the shelf is empty, and keep two weeks in hand ([Medical module](module:medical)).

{{module:food}}

## First 72 hours

- **Fuel.** The register's fuel scenarios are regional: a refinery or terminal lost, replenishment taking "several days", the National Emergency Plan for Fuel and Operation ESCALIN's military tanker drivers keeping priority users supplied ([NRR 2025, p. 49](doc:nrr-2025#page=49); [NRR 2025, p. 69](doc:nrr-2025#page=69)). Petrol at home is limited to 30 litres in approved containers of up to 10 litres plastic or 20 litres metal, stored outside the house ([Petroleum regulations 2014](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/1637/contents); [HSE INDG370, p. 1](doc:hse-indg370#page=1)). Cycle, walk, share cars ([Vehicles and fuel module](module:vehicles-fuel)).
- **Food.** Britain imports a large share of what it eats and runs shops on daily deliveries ([Food security](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Food_security); [Supply chain](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Supply_chain)); fresh produce goes first, then bread and milk, then tins. Cook the fridge and freezer contents first if power is also uncertain ([FSA chill and freeze](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/how-to-chill-freeze-and-defrost-food-safely)).
- **Water** is usually the last thing to stop, but bottled water vanishes with the first rumour; fill containers from the tap now ([Water module](module:water)).
- **Gas bottles, heating oil, wood:** heating oil deliveries fail in the same scenarios as diesel ([NRR 2025, p. 69](doc:nrr-2025#page=69)); order early, share deliveries with neighbours.
- **Share, do not hoard.** Panic buying is the mechanism of the shortage, not a response to it ([Panic buying](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Panic_buying)).

{{module:vehicles-fuel}}

{{module:water}}

## First month

- **Cook from staples.** Rice, pasta, oats, lentils, flour, oil, tinned tomatoes, tinned fish and UHT milk make months of meals; the recipe collections in this box need no fresh ingredients ([Seasoned Advice, cooking Q&A](kiwix:cooking.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions); [Food Preparation Library](kiwix:zimgit-food-preparation_en/home)).
- **Preserve what appears.** A glut of anything is dried, pickled, jammed or canned; low-acid foods must be pressure-canned or they can grow botulism ([USDA canning](kiwix:usda-2015_en/home); [Botulism](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Botulism)).
- **Medicines.** The WHO essential list is what a pharmacy should hold when the wholesalers fail; it is the list to ask about ([WHO EML, p. 12](doc:who-eml-2025#page=12)). Insulin, inhalers, thyroid and heart drugs are the ones to worry about first ([Insulin (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_medicines/www.nhs.uk/medicines/insulin/); [Salbutamol inhaler (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_medicines/www.nhs.uk/medicines/salbutamol-inhaler/)).
- **Grow.** The hungry gap is April and May; anything sown now is food in three months ([Growing food module](module:growing-food)).
- **Fix rather than replace.** Spares stop arriving before food does; the repair guides and the tool kit are the supply chain you control ([Tools and repair module](module:tools-repair); [iFixit](kiwix:ifixit_en_all/home/home)).
- **Community.** A village bulk order, a shared freezer run on one generator, a lift rota: the [community module](module:community) has the template.

{{module:medical}}

{{module:growing-food}}

{{module:tools-repair}}

## Long term

Global supply chains failed for two years after 2020 and the container ship stuck in the Suez Canal in March 2021 held up a tenth of world trade for a week ([2021–2023 global supply chain crisis](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2021–2023_global_supply_chain_crisis); [2021 Suez Canal obstruction](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2021_Suez_Canal_obstruction)). Just-in-time stocking means shops hold days, not weeks ([Lean manufacturing](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Lean_manufacturing)). If the failure lasts a year, the country returns to the wartime pattern: rationing, allotments, local food, repair ([Rationing in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom); [Victory garden](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Victory_garden)); the [famine playbook](playbook:famine) is the next chapter.

{{module:community}}

## UK specifics

- **Fuel plans.** The National Emergency Plan for Fuel, priority-user schemes and Operation ESCALIN ([NRR 2025, p. 49](doc:nrr-2025#page=49)); the Energy Act 1976 powers over production and supply in a real shortage ([NRR 2025, p. 63](doc:nrr-2025#page=63)); the UK's emergency oil stocks released with other International Energy Agency members ([NRR 2025, p. 63](doc:nrr-2025#page=63)).
- **Gas** comes by pipeline from Norway and as LNG; the UK does not use Russian gas but pays European prices, and the heating season is October to May ([NRR 2025, p. 62](doc:nrr-2025#page=62)).
- **Fuel at home:** 30 litres of petrol without notifying anyone, 30 to 275 litres only after notifying the Petroleum Enforcement Authority; diesel is not covered ([Petroleum regulations 2014](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/1637/contents)).
- **Food safety** when shops and fridges fail: the FSA rules on chilling and the 8 °C limit ([FSA chill and freeze](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/how-to-chill-freeze-and-defrost-food-safely)); foraging is lawful for your own use ([Foraging law](page:foraging-law)).
- **Pharmacy First** in England, 111, and the GP's repeat system are the routes to medicines; hoarding prescription drugs is not ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Where the fuel is:** every forecourt is on the map ([fuel overlay](map:?overlay=fuel)); stations near motorway junctions and depots are resupplied first.

## Checklist

- [ ] Stock count on the fridge door: food, medicines, fuel, cash, nappies, pet food, gas {#stock-count}
- [ ] Repeat prescriptions ordered early; alternatives discussed with the pharmacist {#prescriptions-early}
- [ ] Tank above half; petrol stored lawfully outside, 30 litres at most {#fuel-half-tank}
- [ ] Two weeks of staples bought in ordinary amounts; no panic trolley {#two-weeks-staples}
- [ ] Water containers filled and stored in the dark {#water-filled}
- [ ] Bicycle serviced; car-share and lift rota agreed with neighbours {#bike-and-lifts}
- [ ] Freezer contents cooked or preserved first; USDA rules for low-acid canning {#preserve-first}
- [ ] Seeds, seed potatoes and a bed ready; sowing calendar checked {#sow-something}
- [ ] Village bulk order or shared delivery organised {#bulk-order}
- [ ] Spares for the things that break: fuses, tape, wire, filters, tyre patches {#spares}
```

```markdown
reviewed: null

  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Help during a flood (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/help-during-flood
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Prepare for flooding (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/prepare-for-flooding
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: What to do after a flood (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/after-flood
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Flooding and health, advice for the public (UKHSA)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/flooding-and-health-public-advice/flooding-and-health-advice-for-the-public
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Get flood warnings (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/get-flood-warnings
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: HSE INDG317 Chainsaws at work
    doc: hse-indg317
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: North Sea flood of 1953 (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/North_Sea_flood_of_1953
    as_at: 2026-02-15

## Right now

**A severe flood warning means danger to life: act now.** Floodline 0345 988 1188 (England, Scotland, Wales) and 0300 2000 100 in Northern Ireland give the warning in force; a flood alert means prepare, a flood warning means act, a severe flood warning means immediate danger ([Get flood warnings](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/get-flood-warnings)). Move people, pets, medicines, documents and valuables upstairs; turn off gas, electricity and water at the mains if water is coming in; go up, not out, unless told to evacuate ([Help during a flood](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/help-during-flood)).

**Never walk or drive through floodwater:** 15 cm of moving water knocks an adult over, 30 cm floats a car, 60 cm carries it away, and the water hides open manholes and sewage ([Help during a flood](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/help-during-flood)).

**In a storm, stay in.** The register's storm scenario is mean winds over 55 mph and gusts over 85 mph across several regions for six hours, with deaths "mainly due to falling trees, structures or other debris" ([NRR 2025, p. 139](doc:nrr-2025#page=139)). Park away from trees, stay off the coast and sea walls, and keep away from windows.

**Power off?** 105 in Great Britain, NIE Networks 03457 643 643 in Northern Ireland ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).

{{module:evacuation}}

## First 72 hours

- **Expect outages of days.** Storm disruption to power, communications and transport "could last for 1-4 days and for more than 5 days in remote rural locations" ([NRR 2025, p. 139](doc:nrr-2025#page=139)); a river flood takes electricity and telecoms from many homes for days to weeks ([NRR 2025, p. 147](doc:nrr-2025#page=147)); all fixed and mobile communication in a region can go at once, with landlines off for several days ([NRR 2025, p. 88](doc:nrr-2025#page=88)). Run the [grid collapse playbook](playbook:grid-collapse) in parallel.
- **Floodwater is sewage.** Wash hands after any contact, keep children and pets out of it, and throw away food it touched, including tins whose seals were under water ([UKHSA flooding and health](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/flooding-and-health-public-advice/flooding-and-health-advice-for-the-public)). Cuts that meet floodwater get cleaned and watched ([Wound cleaning card](card:wound-cleaning)); fever with muscle pain a week later can be leptospirosis ([Leptospirosis (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/leptospirosis/)).
- **Water supply.** A "do not drink" or boil notice from the water company overrides everything; the 2007 floods put the Mythe works under water and left 350,000 people in Gloucestershire without mains water for up to 17 days ([2007 United Kingdom floods](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2007_United_Kingdom_floods)). Bowsers and bottled water stations prioritise the Priority Services Register ([Water module](module:water)).
- **Pumps and generators** run outdoors only; carbon monoxide from petrol pumps in a flooded cellar kills ([Carbon monoxide card](card:carbon-monoxide)).
- **Cold and wet.** Hypothermia in a flooded house in winter is a real risk; dry clothes, one warm room, hot drinks ([Hypothermia card](card:hypothermia)).
- **Rest centres** are opened by the council; register on arrival so you can be found ([Help during a flood](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/help-during-flood)).

{{module:water}}

{{module:power}}

{{module:shelter-heat}}

{{module:comms}}

## First month

- **Going back in.** Wait until told it is safe; check the structure before entering, never step into standing water where electricity may be live, and have gas and electrics checked before reconnection ([After a flood](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/after-flood)).
- **Clean-up.** Gloves, boots, masks; shovel out mud, hose down, then disinfect hard surfaces; dry the house slowly with ventilation and heat; mould grows within days and is a respiratory hazard ([UKHSA flooding and health](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/flooding-and-health-public-advice/flooding-and-health-advice-for-the-public)). Photograph everything before you throw it away; the insurer needs it ([After a flood](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/after-flood)).
- **Sewage and drains.** Septic tanks and cesspools flood too; do not use them until the water table drops; the rules for drainage fields are in [Approved Document H, p. 31](doc:ad-h#page=31).
- **Chainsaws** clear the trees that took the roads and the lines: chaps, helmet, gloves, never above shoulder height, and the kickback zone ([HSE INDG317, p. 1](doc:hse-indg317#page=1)); a tree under tension is a professional's job.
- **Roofs.** Tarpaulin and battens from inside the loft where you can; never on a roof in wind ([Tools and repair module](module:tools-repair)).
- **Health.** Stress, sleep loss and grief follow floods for months; the flooding health page says so and where to turn ([UKHSA flooding and health](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/flooding-and-health-public-advice/flooding-and-health-advice-for-the-public)).

{{module:sanitation}}

{{module:tools-repair}}

{{module:medical}}

## Long term

The 1953 North Sea surge killed 307 people on the English coast in one night and led to the Thames Barrier and the warning system that exists now ([North Sea flood of 1953](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/North_Sea_flood_of_1953); [Thames Barrier](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Thames_Barrier)); the register's coastal scenario is that flood again along the east coast, with mass evacuation ([NRR 2025, p. 145](doc:nrr-2025#page=145)). Storms are becoming more frequent and intense ([NRR 2025, p. 139](doc:nrr-2025#page=139)). After the clean-up: check the long-term flood risk for the address ([Check long-term flood risk](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/check-long-term-flood-risk)), fit flood doors and non-return valves, move sockets up, and use Flood Re if insurance is refused ([Flood Re](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Flood_Re)). A dam or reservoir above you can fail without warning, as Toddbrook nearly did in 2019 ([NRR 2025, p. 118](doc:nrr-2025#page=118); [Toddbrook Reservoir](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Toddbrook_Reservoir)).

## UK specifics

- **Warnings.** Flood alerts and warnings by phone, text and email from the Environment Agency, Natural Resources Wales and SEPA; sign up now ([Get flood warnings](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/get-flood-warnings)); Northern Ireland's Flooding Incident Line is 0300 2000 100 ([nidirect flooding](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.nidirect.gov.uk/articles/flooding)). Met Office weather warnings are yellow, amber and red ([National Severe Weather Warning Service](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/National_Severe_Weather_Warning_Service)); the Emergency Alert system was used for Storm Darragh in December 2024 ([Wales Resilience Framework, p. 29](doc:wales-resilience-framework-2025#page=29)).
- **Flood zones** on the map: EA zones 2 and 3 for England, NRW for Wales, SEPA for Scotland, DfI Rivers for Northern Ireland ([flood zones overlay](map:?overlay=flood-zones)); there is no data for the Republic of Ireland, the Isle of Man or the Channel Islands on that layer.
- **Surface water** floods are the hardest to warn: 6 to 24 hours' notice at best, worst in the south-east ([NRR 2025, p. 149](doc:nrr-2025#page=149)).
- **Precedents.** The 1987 and Burns Day 1990 storms are the register's model ([NRR 2025, p. 139](doc:nrr-2025#page=139)); Storm Eunice in 2022 brought gusts over 100 mph and three deaths ([NRR 2025, p. 139](doc:nrr-2025#page=139)); Storm Arwen in 2021 and Storm Éowyn in 2025 left homes without power for over a week ([Storm Arwen](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Storm_Arwen); [Storm Éowyn](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Storm_Éowyn)); Storm Desmond in 2015 flooded Carlisle and blacked out Lancaster ([Storm Desmond](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Storm_Desmond)).
- **Sandbags** are the council's to give and yours to fill; they slow water, they do not stop it ([Sandbag](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sandbag); [Prepare for flooding](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/prepare-for-flooding)).
- **Pollution:** oil, chemicals or sewage in floodwater go to the Environment Agency incident hotline 0800 80 70 60 ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Flood warnings signed up for this address; Floodline number on the fridge {#flood-warnings-signed-up}
- [ ] Long-term flood risk for the address checked; flood zone on the map looked at {#flood-risk-checked}
- [ ] Flood kit upstairs: torch, radio, medicines, documents, warm clothes, water, phone charger {#flood-kit-upstairs}
- [ ] Stopcock, gas valve and consumer unit found and labelled {#mains-labelled}
- [ ] Car moved to high ground before the water; never driven through floodwater {#car-high-ground}
- [ ] Sandbags or flood boards fitted; airbricks covered; valuables raised {#defences-fitted}
- [ ] Rest centre location and evacuation route written down {#rest-centre-route}
- [ ] Hands washed after any floodwater contact; food it touched thrown away {#floodwater-hygiene}
- [ ] Pumps and generators outdoors only; CO alarm on {#pumps-outdoors}
- [ ] Every damaged item photographed before disposal; insurer called {#photograph-damage}
- [ ] Electrics and gas checked by a professional before reconnection {#check-before-reconnect}
- [ ] Chainsaw kit complete or the job left to someone with it {#chainsaw-safety}
```
