`playbooks/scenarios/severe-winter.md`:

```markdown
---
id: severe-winter
title: Prolonged severe winter
icon: snowflake
order: 13
summary: A week or more of deep snow and hard frost with heating fuel short. Cold-Health Alerts, 18 degrees in one room, gas and power cuts, frozen pipes, falls.
modules: [shelter-heat, power, food, water, medical, vehicles-fuel, comms, community]
overlays: [health, fuel, rail]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Adverse Weather and Health Plan 2026 to 2027 (UKHSA)
    doc: awhp-2026
    as_at: 2026-03-31
  - title: Keep warm, keep well (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/live-well/seasonal-health/keep-warm-keep-well/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Hypothermia (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/hypothermia/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Cold Weather Payment (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/cold-weather-payment
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Approved Document J
    doc: ad-j
    as_at: 2022-06
  - title: Winter of 1962–1963 in the United Kingdom (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Winter_of_1962–1963_in_the_United_Kingdom
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Winter of 1946–47 in the United Kingdom (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Winter_of_1946–47_in_the_United_Kingdom
    as_at: 2026-02-15
---

## Right now

**Heat one room to 18 °C and live in it.** The NHS figure for anyone over 65, under five or with a long-term condition is at least 18 °C ([Keep warm, keep well (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/live-well/seasonal-health/keep-warm-keep-well/)). Curtains shut at dusk, doors closed, draughts blocked, layers and a hat, hot-water bottles, everyone sleeping in the warm room ([Shelter and heat module](module:shelter-heat)).

**Read the alert.** Cold-Health Alerts run from 1 November to 30 March: yellow means impacts on the vulnerable, amber means the whole health service feels it, red means "significant risk to life for even the healthy population" ([AWHP, p. 34](doc:awhp-2026#page=34)). The register's scenario is snow over 30 cm lying for at least seven days across lowland Britain, daily means below minus 3 °C and nights below minus 10 °C, with falls, road accidents, hypothermia and excess deaths ([NRR 2025, p. 143](doc:nrr-2025#page=143)).

**Do not** bring a barbecue, generator, patio heater or camping stove indoors, and do not run the car in the garage to warm it: carbon monoxide kills in a shut house ([Carbon monoxide card](card:carbon-monoxide)).

**Falls** are the commonest injury: grit the path, wear boots with grip, and keep older people in until it thaws ([NRR 2025, p. 143](doc:nrr-2025#page=143)).

{{module:shelter-heat}}

## First 72 hours

- **Hypothermia** starts with shivering and clumsiness and ends with drowsiness and no shivering; warm the trunk first, no alcohol, no hot bath, 999 for confusion ([Hypothermia (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/hypothermia/); [Hypothermia card](card:hypothermia)). Frostbite and chilblains are the outdoor injuries ([Frostbite (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/frostbite/)).
- **Fuel.** Gas boilers stop without electricity ([What still works](page:what-still-works)); heating oil and LPG deliveries fail on snowbound roads ([NRR 2025, p. 69](doc:nrr-2025#page=69)); if gas supply itself fails in winter the plan is 3-hour rolling power cuts while domestic gas is protected ([NRR 2025, p. 94](doc:nrr-2025#page=94)). Ration fuel to the warm room from day one.
- **Stoves.** A wood or solid-fuel stove needs a working flue, an air supply and a carbon monoxide alarm ([Approved Document J, p. 43](doc:ad-j#page=43)); chimneys unused for years cause chimney fires ([Chimney fire](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Chimney_fire)). Paraffin and LPG heaters need a window open a crack ([Kerosene heater](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Kerosene_heater)).
- **Pipes.** Keep a trickle running in the coldest pipe, lag what you can reach, and know the stopcock; a burst when it thaws floods the house ([Tools and repair module](module:tools-repair)).
- **Food and drink.** Warm food and drinks matter; oats, soup, stew from the store ([Food module](module:food)). A fridge is not needed: a north-facing porch is one.
- **Phones and power.** Snow brings down lines; a regional outage in winter is the register's worst case for the electricity network ([NRR 2025, p. 92](doc:nrr-2025#page=92)); 105 and the [grid collapse playbook](playbook:grid-collapse).

{{module:power}}

{{module:medical}}

{{module:food}}

## First month

- **Water.** Mains pipes freeze and burst under roads; standpipes and bowsers follow; store and treat as in the [water module](module:water); melted snow is water once boiled.
- **Vehicles.** Winter kit in the boot: blanket, shovel, grit, torch, food, water, charger, snow socks or chains; do not set out in a red warning; a stuck car is a shelter, so stay with it and run the engine ten minutes an hour with the exhaust clear of snow ([Vehicles and fuel module](module:vehicles-fuel); [Snow chains](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Snow_chains)).
- **Money.** Cold Weather Payments are paid automatically to eligible households for each seven-day period of mean temperature at or below 0 °C between 1 November and 31 March ([Cold Weather Payment](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/cold-weather-payment)); Winter Fuel Payments and the Priority Services Register cover the rest ([Priority Services Register](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.thepsr.co.uk/)).
- **Neighbours.** The people who die in a cold spell are older, alone and indoors; a daily knock is the intervention ([Community module](module:community); [AWHP, p. 22](doc:awhp-2026#page=22)).
- **Illness.** Flu, pneumonia and heart attacks rise in cold weather; the cards and the NHS pages are in the box ([Flu (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/flu/); [Heart attack card](card:heart-attack)).

{{module:water}}

{{module:vehicles-fuel}}

{{module:community}}

## Long term

The winter of 1962 to 1963 froze Britain from Boxing Day to March, the sea iced over at Herne Bay and the ground stayed frozen for two months ([Winter of 1962–1963 in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Winter_of_1962–1963_in_the_United_Kingdom)); 1947 added a coal shortage, power cuts and ration cuts to the snow ([Winter of 1946–1947 in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Winter_of_1946–47_in_the_United_Kingdom)). A house that has been through one is insulated to current standards ([Approved Document L volume 1](doc:ad-l1)), has a stove with a safe flue and a season's fuel ([Approved Document J, p. 11](doc:ad-j#page=11)), and a community that has a warm hub with a generator ([Wales Resilience Framework, p. 29](doc:wales-resilience-framework-2025#page=29)). A winter that does not end is the [impact winter playbook](playbook:impact-winter).

{{module:comms}}

## UK specifics

- **Alerts.** Cold-Health Alerts (1 November to 30 March) come from UKHSA and the Met Office and are cascaded to the public through the news ([AWHP, p. 34](doc:awhp-2026#page=34); [AWHP, p. 22](doc:awhp-2026#page=22)); Met Office snow and ice warnings are yellow, amber and red ([National Severe Weather Warning Service](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/National_Severe_Weather_Warning_Service)).
- **The 2018 "Beast from the East"** is the register's reference event: transport disruption, school closures and power cuts across the UK ([NRR 2025, p. 143](doc:nrr-2025#page=143); [2018 British Isles cold wave](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2018_British_Isles_cold_wave)).
- **Gas safety:** the gas emergency line is 0800 111 999 in Great Britain and 0800 002 001 in Northern Ireland; a smell of gas means windows open, no switches, out, and the call ([HSE domestic gas](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.hse.gov.uk/gas/domestic/index.htm); [UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Support:** Cold Weather Payments, Winter Fuel Payments, the Priority Services Register, and council warm hubs ([Cold Weather Payment](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/cold-weather-payment)).
- **Where the help is:** hospitals, pharmacies and open fuel stations on the map ([health and fuel overlay](map:?overlay=health&overlay=fuel)); rail runs on diesel where the wires are down ([rail overlay](map:?overlay=rail)).
- **Extreme weather** guidance for every hazard is on one GOV.UK page ([Extreme weather and natural hazards](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/extreme-weather-and-natural-hazards)).

## Checklist

- [ ] One warm room chosen; 18 °C target; thermometer in it {#warm-room-18}
- [ ] Curtains shut at dusk, doors closed, draughts blocked, hats and layers on {#curtains-and-layers}
- [ ] CO alarm tested; nothing burning indoors that should be outdoors {#co-alarm-tested}
- [ ] Stove flue swept and checked; a week of fuel by the stove {#stove-and-fuel}
- [ ] Stopcock found; exposed pipes lagged; a trickle running in the coldest one {#pipes-protected}
- [ ] Path gritted; boots with grip; older people kept in until the thaw {#grit-and-boots}
- [ ] Warm food, oats, soup and stock for two weeks {#warm-food-store}
- [ ] Winter kit in the car; no journeys in a red warning {#car-winter-kit}
- [ ] Neighbours over 65 or alone knocked on every day {#daily-knock}
- [ ] Cold Weather Payment eligibility and PSR registration checked {#payments-and-psr}
- [ ] Prescriptions for a fortnight collected before the snow {#prescriptions-collected}

## Go deeper

- [Shelter and heat module](module:shelter-heat)
- [Hypothermia card](card:hypothermia)
- [Carbon monoxide card](card:carbon-monoxide)
- [Adverse Weather and Health Plan, the alert levels](doc:awhp-2026#page=34)
- [Approved Document J, carbon monoxide alarms](doc:ad-j#page=43)
- [Winter of 1962–1963 (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Winter_of_1962–1963_in_the_United_Kingdom)
- [Wood-burning stove (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Wood-burning_stove)
- [Winter weather (Ready.gov)](kiwix:www.ready.gov_en/www.ready.gov/winter-weather)
- [The Great Outdoors Q&A](kiwix:outdoors.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)
- [Hospitals and fuel on the map](map:?overlay=health&overlay=fuel)
- [Grid collapse playbook](playbook:grid-collapse)
- [Impact winter playbook](playbook:impact-winter)
```

`playbooks/scenarios/heat-drought.md`:

```markdown
---
id: heat-drought
title: Heatwave, drought and water failure
icon: thermometer
order: 14
summary: Five days over 35 degrees, three dry winters, hosepipe bans, then standpipes and bowsers. Cooling people, saving water, wildfire, and growing food in a drought.
modules: [water, medical, shelter-heat, food, growing-food, livestock, community]
overlays: [water, health, flood-zones]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Adverse Weather and Health Plan 2026 to 2027 (UKHSA)
    doc: awhp-2026
    as_at: 2026-03-31
  - title: Beat the heat (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/beat-the-heat-hot-weather-advice
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Heat exhaustion and heatstroke (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/heat-exhaustion-heatstroke/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Drinking water for consumers (DWI)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.dwi.gov.uk/consumers/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: 2022 United Kingdom heatwaves (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2022_United_Kingdom_heatwaves
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: 1976 British Isles heatwave (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/1976_British_Isles_heatwave
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Approved Document O
    doc: ad-o
    as_at: 2021-12
---

## Right now

**Cool the people, then the house.** Heat-Health Alerts run from 1 June to 30 September; a red alert is "significant risk to life for even the healthy population" ([AWHP, p. 34](doc:awhp-2026#page=34)). The register's scenario is five days above 35 °C, approaching or passing 40 °C in south-eastern, eastern and central England, affecting 50 to 70% of the population, with excess deaths and failures of transport, power and water ([NRR 2025, p. 141](doc:nrr-2025#page=141)); it happened at Coningsby on 19 July 2022 at 40.3 °C ([2022 United Kingdom heatwaves](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2022_United_Kingdom_heatwaves)).

**Heat exhaustion** (sweating, dizziness, cramps, nausea) is cooled and rehydrated within 30 minutes; **heatstroke** (hot skin, confusion, fits, no better after 30 minutes) is 999 and cold water on the skin now ([Heat exhaustion and heatstroke (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/heat-exhaustion-heatstroke/); [Heat stroke card](card:heat-stroke)). Babies, older people and anyone on diuretics or mental-health medicines are the risk group ([Beat the heat](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/beat-the-heat-hot-weather-advice)).

**The house:** blinds and curtains shut on the sunny side by day, windows open at night when it is cooler outside than in, sleep on the lowest floor, wet sheets and a fan, no cooking with the oven ([Beat the heat](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/beat-the-heat-hot-weather-advice)).

**Water:** if the taps have stopped or a notice is out, use the stored water and find the bowser or bottled water station the company has announced; the Priority Services Register gets deliveries ([Priority Services Register](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.thepsr.co.uk/)).

{{module:medical}}

## First 72 hours

- **Drink before thirst;** pale urine is the target; two to three litres a day for an adult in heat, more with work; oral rehydration solution for anyone dizzy or cramping ([Dehydration card](card:dehydration); [Dehydration (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/dehydration/)).
- **Food safety.** Fridges struggle above 30 °C; keep them shut and full; chilled food above 8 °C for more than four hours is thrown away ([FSA chill and freeze](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/how-to-chill-freeze-and-defrost-food-safely)); food poisoning rises with the temperature ([Food poisoning (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/food-poisoning/)).
- **Power.** Demand for cooling and sagging lines bring outages in the heat ([NRR 2025, p. 141](doc:nrr-2025#page=141)); the [grid collapse playbook](playbook:grid-collapse) applies with the fridge as the priority.
- **Wildfire.** No barbecues or fires in the open, no glass left in grass; a fire in the heath or the field next to houses is 999 and leaving by the road away from the smoke ([Wildfire](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Wildfire); [NRR 2025, p. 127](doc:nrr-2025#page=127)); London lost houses to grass fires on 19 July 2022 ([2022 United Kingdom wildfires](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2022_United_Kingdom_wildfires)).
- **Water rationing.** Drinking, cooking, hand washing first; wash in a bowl and use it on the garden; flush with grey water ([Water module](module:water)). Do not drink from ponds, rivers or reservoirs: in a hot dry spell they carry blue-green algae ([Cyanobacteria](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cyanobacteria)).
- **Air quality.** Ozone and particulates rise in hot still weather; asthma and COPD sufferers keep inhalers close and stay in at midday ([NRR 2025, p. 153](doc:nrr-2025#page=153); [Asthma (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/asthma/)).

{{module:water}}

{{module:shelter-heat}}

{{module:food}}

## First month

- **Drought.** A severe drought needs three consecutive dry winters; the register places it in south and east England with public supply restrictions, farm losses and a fire risk the fire service cannot fully fight for lack of water ([NRR 2025, p. 151](doc:nrr-2025#page=151)). Hosepipe bans come first ([Outdoor water-use restriction](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Outdoor_water-use_restriction)); the 1976 drought went as far as standpipes in streets ([1976 British Isles heatwave](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/1976_British_Isles_heatwave); [Standpipe (street)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Standpipe_(street))).
- **Supply failure.** The register's water scenario is the sudden loss of piped water or water "unfit for human consumption even after boiling" in one region, with alternative supplies prioritising the vulnerable ([NRR 2025, p. 120](doc:nrr-2025#page=120)). Water companies must supply bottled water and bowsers ([DWI consumers](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.dwi.gov.uk/consumers/)).
- **Storing and harvesting.** Every water butt filled from every downpipe ([Rainwater harvesting](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rainwater_harvesting); [Approved Document G](doc:ad-g)); rainwater is for the garden and the toilet unless treated ([Water disinfection](page:water-disinfection)). Cover butts against algae and mosquitoes. Legionella grows in warm stagnant water; run hoses and showers that have stood in the sun before using them ([Legionnaires' disease (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/legionnaires-disease/)).
- **Growing.** Mulch, water at the roots in the evening, shade seedlings, choose drought-tolerant crops; the gardening Q&A has the detail ([Growing food module](module:growing-food)). Goats need 5 to 10 litres a day, hens about 0.3; shade and water for every animal ([Livestock module](module:livestock)).
- **After the heat: flash floods.** Thunderstorms on baked ground flood roads and homes with 6 to 24 hours' warning ([NRR 2025, p. 149](doc:nrr-2025#page=149); [NRR 2025, p. 141](doc:nrr-2025#page=141)); the flood zones layer and the [storms and flooding playbook](playbook:storms-flooding).

{{module:growing-food}}

{{module:livestock}}

## Long term

Heatwaves are becoming more likely and mortality "increases significantly with increasing temperatures" ([NRR 2025, p. 141](doc:nrr-2025#page=141)); hotter summers, higher demand and a growing population make drought more likely ([NRR 2025, p. 151](doc:nrr-2025#page=151)). The lasting fixes are external shading, cross-ventilation and insulation designed against overheating ([Approved Document O](doc:ad-o)), rainwater and grey-water systems ([Greywater](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Greywater)), a garden planned for dry summers, and a street that checks on its older residents every hot day ([Community module](module:community)). Private supplies (wells and boreholes) are the owner's to test and treat ([DWI private supplies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.dwi.gov.uk/private-water-supplies/)).

{{module:community}}

## UK specifics

- **Alerts** come from UKHSA and the Met Office through the Weather-Health Alert system ([Weather-health alerting system](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/weather-health-alerting-system); [AWHP, p. 22](doc:awhp-2026#page=22)); the government's heat pages are collected in one place ([Heat collection (GOV.UK)](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/collections/heat)).
- **Restrictions** on water use are made under the Water Industry Act 1991 and drought orders ([Water Industry Act 1991](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1991/56/contents)); breaking a hosepipe ban is an offence.
- **Who to call:** your water company's number from the bill; the Environment Agency incident line 0800 80 70 60 for pollution and fish kills; 999 for wildfire and heatstroke ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Reservoirs and water works** are on the map ([water overlay](map:?overlay=water)); the flood zones layer shows where the storm after the heat will go ([flood zones overlay](map:?overlay=flood-zones)).
- **Sunburn and skin:** cover up, factor 30, shade at midday; a burn that blisters is a burn ([Sunburn (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/sunburn/); [Burns card](card:burns)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Blinds shut by day on the sunny side; windows open at night; sleep low {#shade-and-ventilate}
- [ ] Two to three litres of water a person a day drunk; rehydration salts to hand {#drink-enough}
- [ ] Older neighbours and anyone alone checked twice a day in a red alert {#check-vulnerable}
- [ ] Fridge kept shut and full; chilled food above 8 °C for four hours thrown away {#fridge-discipline}
- [ ] Water butts filled and covered; grey water saved for the garden and the toilet {#butts-and-greywater}
- [ ] Stored drinking water for three days; bowser and bottled water station located {#drinking-water-stored}
- [ ] No fires or barbecues outdoors; wildfire escape route known {#no-open-fires}
- [ ] Inhalers and heart medicines within reach; midday indoors {#medicines-midday}
- [ ] Garden mulched; animals shaded and watered {#mulch-and-shade}
- [ ] Flood kit ready for the thunderstorm that follows {#flood-after-heat}

## Go deeper

- [Water module](module:water)
- [Heat stroke card](card:heat-stroke)
- [Dehydration card](card:dehydration)
- [Beat the heat (GOV.UK)](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/beat-the-heat-hot-weather-advice)
- [Adverse Weather and Health Plan, heat](doc:awhp-2026#page=34)
- [2022 United Kingdom heatwaves (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2022_United_Kingdom_heatwaves)
- [Drought in the United Kingdom (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Drought_in_the_United_Kingdom)
- [Extreme heat (Ready.gov)](kiwix:www.ready.gov_en/www.ready.gov/extreme-heat)
- [Water Treatment Library](kiwix:zimgit-water_en/home)
- [Reservoirs and water works on the map](map:?overlay=water&overlay=health)
- [Famine playbook](playbook:famine)
```

`playbooks/scenarios/volcanic.md`:

```markdown
---
id: volcanic
title: Volcanic ash and gas
icon: mountain
order: 15
summary: An Icelandic eruption sends ash and sulphur over Britain for weeks. Bad-air days, masks, covered water, livestock indoors, no flights, a cold summer.
modules: [shelter-heat, medical, water, food, livestock, growing-food, comms]
overlays: [health, airports-military]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Laki (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Laki
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: 2010 eruptions of Eyjafjallajökull (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2010_eruptions_of_Eyjafjallajökull
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Volcanic ash (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_ash
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Year Without a Summer (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Year_Without_a_Summer
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Extreme weather and natural hazards (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/extreme-weather-and-natural-hazards
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Asthma (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/asthma/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Fluoride toxicity (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fluoride_toxicity
    as_at: 2026-02-15
---

## Right now

**Iceland is the source.** The register names Bárðarbunga and Eyjafjallajökull; its scenario is an ash-rich eruption lasting three months with up to 15 days of severe flight disruption and 10 more of moderate ([NRR 2025, p. 129](doc:nrr-2025#page=129)). In 2010 Eyjafjallajökull closed British airspace for six days ([2010 eruptions of Eyjafjallajökull](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2010_eruptions_of_Eyjafjallajökull)). Nobody is flying: plan accordingly.

**A gas-rich eruption is the dangerous one.** Laki in 1783 sent a sulphurous haze over Britain for months; the summer of 1783 killed an estimated 23,000 people in England, mostly from breathing it ([Laki](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Laki)). The register's air-quality scenario, 30 days of elevated ozone or fine particulates with deaths from respiratory and heart disease, is the planning frame ([NRR 2025, p. 153](doc:nrr-2025#page=153)).

**On a bad-air day:** stay in, windows shut, exertion off, inhalers close; anyone with asthma or COPD who cannot speak in sentences is 999 ([Asthma (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/asthma/); [Asthma attack card](card:asthma-attack)). Sulphur dioxide stings eyes and throat and is worse for children and the old ([Sulfur dioxide](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sulfur_dioxide)).

**Cover the water.** Ash and its fluoride settle on butts, troughs and open tanks; lids on now ([Volcanic ash](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_ash)).

{{module:medical}}

## First 72 hours

- **Masks.** An FFP2 or FFP3 respirator stops ash; a cloth over the face stops the largest grains and nothing else; nothing stops sulphur dioxide except staying in ([European respirator standards](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/European_respirator_standards); [Surgical mask](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Surgical_mask)). Goggles for anyone who must work outside; contact lenses out.
- **The house.** Doors and windows shut, gaps taped, damp cloths at the sills, extractor fans off; the boiler's air intake and the car's cabin filter clog with ash ([Volcanic ash](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_ash)). Sweep ash wet, never dry; do not wash it into drains.
- **Roofs.** Wet ash is heavy; more than a few centimetres on a flat roof or a conservatory is a collapse risk; clear it from the ground or not at all ([Volcanic ash](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_ash)).
- **Driving.** Ash cuts visibility and scours engines and brakes; stay off the road in ashfall; if you must, slow, lights on, windows up, air on recirculate.
- **Animals.** Grazing animals in Iceland died of fluorosis from ash on the grass in 1783 ([Fluoride toxicity](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fluoride_toxicity); [Laki](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Laki)); bring livestock and poultry under cover, feed stored hay and covered water, keep pets in ([Livestock module](module:livestock)).
- **Warnings.** UKHSA and the Met Office announce bad-air days through the Daily Air Quality Index and Emergency Alerts where needed ([Air quality index](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Air_quality_index); [How alerts work](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/alerts/how-alerts-work)).

{{module:shelter-heat}}

{{module:water}}

{{module:livestock}}

## First month

- **Weeks of it.** A three-month eruption means episodes of ashfall and haze whenever the wind is from the north-west; keep the mask routine and the covered-water routine for the duration ([NRR 2025, p. 129](doc:nrr-2025#page=129)).
- **Supplies.** No flights means no air freight: some medicines, some fresh produce and mail order stop; the [supply chain playbook](playbook:supply-chain) applies to those things.
- **Water.** Tap water from treated mains is safe; private supplies, butts and troughs need the ash settled out and filtered before disinfection ([Water module](module:water); [DWI private supplies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.dwi.gov.uk/private-water-supplies/)). Acid rain from sulphur dioxide is a garden and pond problem, not a drinking one ([Acid rain](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Acid_rain)).
- **Growing.** A dusting of ash is fertiliser once washed in; a thick fall smothers; rinse leafy crops before eating and cover cold frames ([Growing food module](module:growing-food)).
- **Health.** Chest infections follow weeks of bad air; pneumonia signs and the sepsis rule are on the NHS pages ([Pneumonia (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/pneumonia/); [Sepsis (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/sepsis/)).

{{module:food}}

{{module:growing-food}}

## Long term

A large enough eruption cools the world for a year or two: Tambora in 1815 gave Europe the "Year Without a Summer" in 1816, with frosts in July, failed harvests and famine ([Year Without a Summer](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Year_Without_a_Summer); [1815 eruption of Mount Tambora](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/1815_eruption_of_Mount_Tambora)). If the summer after the eruption is cold and dark, the [impact winter playbook](playbook:impact-winter) and the [famine playbook](playbook:famine) are the next chapters: store this year's harvest, plan next year's for cold, and keep the animals fed.

{{module:comms}}

## UK specifics

- **The register** lists volcanic eruption among natural hazards with Iceland as the main source and aviation as the main impact ([NRR 2025, p. 129](doc:nrr-2025#page=129)); the GOV.UK hazards guidance covers volcanic ash ([Extreme weather and natural hazards](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/extreme-weather-and-natural-hazards)).
- **Which volcanoes:** Bárðarbunga (whose Holuhraun fissure poured sulphur dioxide in 2014 to 2015), Eyjafjallajökull, Grímsvötn, Katla and Hekla are the frequent ones; Laki and Eldgjá are the historic gas eruptions ([Bárðarbunga](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Bárðarbunga); [Grímsvötn](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Grímsvötn); [Katla (volcano)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Katla_(volcano)); [Eldgjá](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Eldgjá)).
- **Airports** on the map close first and open last ([airports overlay](map:?overlay=airports-military)); ferries and the tunnel keep running.
- **Health warnings** come through the Weather-Health Alert system and the air quality index ([Weather-health alerting system](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/weather-health-alerting-system)); 111 for breathing problems that are not an emergency, 999 when they are ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Scotland and the north** are downwind first and most; the Northern Isles and the Hebrides got the worst of Laki's haze ([Laki](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Laki)).

## Checklist

- [ ] FFP2 or FFP3 masks and goggles for every adult; children kept in on bad-air days {#masks-and-goggles}
- [ ] Doors, windows and vents shut; damp cloths at sills; extractors off in ashfall {#seal-the-house}
- [ ] Inhalers and heart medicines to hand; asthma plan written {#inhalers-ready}
- [ ] Water butts, troughs and tanks covered; stored drinking water for three days {#cover-water}
- [ ] Livestock and poultry under cover on stored feed; pets in {#animals-in}
- [ ] Ash swept wet from paths; roofs cleared from the ground or left {#ash-wet-sweep}
- [ ] Car left in the garage; cabin and engine filters checked after ashfall {#car-filters}
- [ ] Air-freighted medicines and essentials reordered early {#air-freight-gap}
- [ ] Leafy crops rinsed; cold frames covered {#rinse-crops}
- [ ] Harvest stored and next year's planting planned for a cold summer {#plan-cold-summer}

## Go deeper

- [Medical module](module:medical)
- [Asthma attack card](card:asthma-attack)
- [Laki (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Laki)
- [Volcanic ash (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_ash)
- [Volcanic winter (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_winter)
- [Volcanic ash and aviation safety (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_ash_and_aviation_safety)
- [Volcanoes (Ready.gov)](kiwix:www.ready.gov_en/www.ready.gov/volcanoes)
- [Earth science Q&A](kiwix:earthscience.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)
- [NRR 2025, volcanic eruption](doc:nrr-2025#page=129)
- [Airports on the map](map:?overlay=airports-military&overlay=health)
- [Impact winter playbook](playbook:impact-winter)
```

`playbooks/scenarios/chemical.md`:

```markdown
---
id: chemical
title: Chemical or industrial disaster
icon: flask
order: 16
summary: A toxic plume from a refinery, chemical works, tanker or attack. Upwind and indoors, seal the room, remove-remove-remove, and the checks afterwards.
modules: [evacuation, medical, water, shelter-heat, comms, sanitation]
overlays: [chemical-sites, health, water]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Chemical emergencies, information for the public (UKHSA)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/chemical-emergencies-information-for-the-public
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: "Ship Captain's Medical Guide, chapter 2: toxic hazards of chemicals"
    doc: scmg-ch02
    as_at: 2019-10
  - title: HSE INDG258 Confined spaces
    doc: hse-indg258
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Buncefield fire (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Buncefield_fire
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Flixborough disaster (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Flixborough_disaster
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Hazchem (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hazchem
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Poisoning (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/poisoning/
    as_at: 2026-09
---

## Right now

**Go in, stay in, tune in.** That is the government's instruction for a chemical release: indoors, doors and windows shut, fans, extractors and the boiler off, into a room with the fewest openings, and the radio on ([UKHSA chemical emergencies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/chemical-emergencies-information-for-the-public)). If you are outside, move upwind or across the wind, uphill, away from the smell; do not drive into it.

**If it is on you: remove, remove, remove.** Get away from the source, take the clothing off (cut it rather than pulling it over the head), blot the skin dry, then wash with plenty of water; bag the clothes ([Chemical exposure card](card:chemical-exposure); [UKHSA chemical emergencies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/chemical-emergencies-information-for-the-public)).

**Do not go into a cellar, tank, pit or building to rescue someone who has collapsed:** the gas that dropped them drops you; confined-space deaths are usually the rescuers ([HSE INDG258](doc:hse-indg258)).

**Which gas?** Chlorine and most industrial gases are heavier than air and pool in low ground, cellars and ground floors; go up ([Chlorine](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Chlorine)). Ammonia is lighter and rises ([Ammonia](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Ammonia)). A tanker's orange Hazchem plate tells the fire service what it carries; the letter E on it means evacuate ([Hazchem](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hazchem)).

{{module:evacuation}}

## First 72 hours

- **How long.** The register's toxic-release scenario is a large gas release from a COMAH site near a town, with deaths, casualties and long-term health effects in the vulnerable ([NRR 2025, p. 108](doc:nrr-2025#page=108)); its fire scenario is Buncefield-style, a visible plume and buildings damaged near the site ([NRR 2025, p. 106](doc:nrr-2025#page=106)). A gas cloud passes in hours; a fire's smoke can last days.
- **Sealing the room.** Tape or wet towels along door and window gaps; one room is easier to seal than a house; open it up as soon as the all-clear comes because a sealed room runs out of air in hours ([Shelter-in-place](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Shelter-in-place)).
- **Symptoms and treatment.** Stinging eyes, cough, chest tightness, headache; fresh air, eye flushing for 15 minutes, no vomiting induced, and 999 for breathing difficulty, burns or confusion, saying what the substance was ([Poisoning (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/poisoning/); [Ship Captain's Medical Guide ch. 2](doc:scmg-ch02)). Chemical burns are rinsed for 20 minutes or more ([Burns card](card:burns)). Lung injury from chlorine or smoke can appear hours later; anyone exposed is watched overnight ([Chemical exposure card](card:chemical-exposure)).
- **Water.** Cover water butts; do not drink from open sources downwind; tap water is safe unless the company says otherwise ([Water module](module:water)).
- **Evacuation** only when the police or an Emergency Alert say so, by the route given; the cordon may be a kilometre or more ([NRR 2025, p. 22](doc:nrr-2025#page=22); [How alerts work](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/alerts/how-alerts-work)).
- **Pets** in; livestock under cover and off contaminated grass ([Evacuation module](module:evacuation)).

{{module:medical}}

{{module:shelter-heat}}

{{module:water}}

## First month

- **Going back.** Ventilate fully when told, wash hard surfaces, launder everything that was exposed, and discard open food and garden produce the plume passed over until the council says otherwise ([UKHSA chemical emergencies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/chemical-emergencies-information-for-the-public)).
- **Health follow-up.** Register with the council's incident helpline so you are in the health study; keep a note of symptoms; asthma flares are common for weeks ([Asthma (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/asthma/)).
- **Deliberate release.** Salisbury in 2018 showed what a nerve agent does and that one contaminated object can kill weeks later ([Poisoning of Sergei and Yulia Skripal](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Poisoning_of_Sergei_and_Yulia_Skripal); [NRR 2025, p. 40](doc:nrr-2025#page=40)); do not pick up discarded bottles, perfume or containers near any incident. Nerve agents cause pinpoint pupils, drooling, twitching and collapse; the treatment is atropine, which paramedics carry ([Nerve agent](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nerve_agent); [Atropine](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Atropine)).
- **Sanitation.** Wash water from decontamination is contaminated; keep it out of the drinking supply and the vegetable patch ([Sanitation module](module:sanitation)).

{{module:comms}}

{{module:sanitation}}

## Long term

Britain's worst industrial accidents, Flixborough in 1974 with 28 dead and Buncefield in 2005, the largest peacetime fire in Europe, produced the COMAH rules that make every major site plan for the public around it ([Flixborough disaster](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Flixborough_disaster); [Buncefield fire](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Buncefield_fire); [Control of Major Accident Hazards Regulations 2015](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Control_of_Major_Accident_Hazards_Regulations_2015)). Bhopal in 1984 is what happens when there is no plan ([Bhopal disaster](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Bhopal_disaster)). Long-term contamination of land and water is the council's and the Environment Agency's to test; keep every letter and result.

## UK specifics

- **The sites.** Refineries at Grangemouth, Stanlow, Fawley and the Humber, the chemical works of Teesside, Runcorn and Ellesmere Port, fuel terminals and gas storage, all on the map ([chemical sites overlay](map:?overlay=chemical-sites&overlay=water); [Grangemouth Refinery](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Grangemouth_Refinery); [Stanlow Oil Refinery](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Stanlow_Oil_Refinery)). Upper-tier COMAH sites must inform the people around them of what to do; ask the site or the council for the leaflet ([NRR 2025, p. 108](doc:nrr-2025#page=108)).
- **Roads and rail.** A tanker or a rail wagon is a moving chemical site; the register plans for accidents with high-consequence dangerous goods ([Dangerous goods](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Dangerous_goods)); the Hazchem plate is the label ([Hazchem](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hazchem)).
- **Numbers:** 999 for the incident; 111 for advice; the Environment Agency incident hotline 0800 80 70 60 for pollution of water or land ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Warnings:** Emergency Alerts, site sirens where they exist, and the police door to door ([Prepare, alerts](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/alerts/)).
- **Household chemicals** cause most poisonings in Britain; bleach and acid cleaners mixed make chlorine gas indoors ([Poisoning (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/poisoning/); [Chlorine](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Chlorine)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Nearest chemical, fuel and refinery sites found on the map; wind direction noted {#sites-and-wind}
- [ ] Shelter room chosen: upstairs, few windows, tape and towels stored in it {#shelter-room}
- [ ] Doors, windows, fans, extractors and the boiler off; radio on {#go-in-stay-in}
- [ ] Anyone exposed: clothes off and bagged, skin blotted then washed, eyes flushed {#remove-remove-remove}
- [ ] Nobody enters a cellar, tank or building to rescue a collapsed person {#no-confined-space-rescue}
- [ ] Water butts covered; open-source water not drunk {#cover-water}
- [ ] Pets in; livestock under cover {#animals-in}
- [ ] Evacuate only when told, by the route given {#evacuate-when-told}
- [ ] Symptoms and times written down for everyone exposed {#symptom-log}
- [ ] After the all-clear: ventilate, wash surfaces, launder, discard exposed food {#clean-up-after}

## Go deeper

- [Chemical exposure card](card:chemical-exposure)
- [Evacuation module](module:evacuation)
- [UKHSA chemical emergencies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/chemical-emergencies-information-for-the-public)
- [Ship Captain's Medical Guide, chemical hazards](doc:scmg-ch02)
- [HSE INDG258 Confined spaces](doc:hse-indg258)
- [Buncefield fire (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Buncefield_fire)
- [Decontamination (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Decontamination)
- [Chemistry Q&A](kiwix:chemistry.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)
- [Hazardous materials incidents (Ready.gov)](kiwix:www.ready.gov_en/www.ready.gov/hazardous-materials-incidents)
- [Chemical sites on the map](map:?overlay=chemical-sites&overlay=health)
- [Terrorism playbook](playbook:terrorism)
```

`playbooks/scenarios/famine.md`:

```markdown
---
id: famine
title: Agricultural collapse and famine
icon: wheat
order: 17
summary: Blight, livestock disease, no fertiliser, no imports. Rationing at home, growing and keeping food, foraging within the law, and the medicine of hunger.
modules: [food, growing-food, livestock, medical, community, water, security-law]
overlays: [water, access-land, footpaths]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Rationing in the United Kingdom (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Great Famine (Ireland) (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Great_Famine_(Ireland)
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Haber process (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Haber_process
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Guidance for safe foraging (FSA)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/guidance-for-safe-foraging/guidance-for-safe-foraging
    as_at: 2023-01-27
  - title: Malnutrition (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/malnutrition/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: The Eatwell Guide (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/live-well/eat-well/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: USDA Complete Guide to Home Canning
    kiwix: usda-2015_en/home
    as_at: 2025-04-11
---

## Right now

**Count the calories, not the tins.** An adult needs about 2,000 kcal a day for women and 2,500 for men ([The Eatwell Guide (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/live-well/eat-well/)); add up what the store holds in days at that rate and ration from today, because a store spent in the first month is gone before the first harvest ([Food module](module:food)). Children, pregnant women and the sick are fed first.

**Plant now, whatever the month.** Potatoes give 2 to 3 kg a square metre; anything sown today is food in three months; the hungry gap is April and May ([Growing food module](module:growing-food)). The 1940 Dig for Victory campaign turned lawns and parks into 1.4 million allotments ([Victory garden](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Victory_garden)).

**Do not eat what you cannot name.** Hemlock water dropwort and death cap kill every year; no fungus on the strength of a photograph, and nothing from roadsides, dog areas or sprayed fields ([FSA safe foraging](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/guidance-for-safe-foraging/guidance-for-safe-foraging)).

{{module:food}}

## First 72 hours

- **Why it happens here.** The register's food and farming risks are contamination of the food chain ([NRR 2025, p. 122](doc:nrr-2025#page=122)), foot and mouth, with 1.9 million animals culled on 2,900 premises in the planning case and six million in 2001 ([NRR 2025, p. 160](doc:nrr-2025#page=160)), avian influenza with eight million birds lost over six to eight months ([NRR 2025, p. 162](doc:nrr-2025#page=162)), African swine fever ([NRR 2025, p. 166](doc:nrr-2025#page=166)), plant pests ([NRR 2025, p. 168](doc:nrr-2025#page=168)) and three-dry-winter drought ([NRR 2025, p. 151](doc:nrr-2025#page=151); [heat and drought playbook](playbook:heat-drought)). Nitrogen fertiliser is made from natural gas by the Haber process, so a gas crisis is a harvest crisis a year later ([Haber process](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Haber_process); [NRR 2025, p. 62](doc:nrr-2025#page=62)).
- **Ration.** The wartime ration was small and the population was healthier for it: registration with one shop, points for tins, and fair shares ([Rationing in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom)). Run the household the same way: a weekly allowance per person, written down.
- **Preserve every glut.** Drying, salting, pickling, jam; low-acid vegetables and meat only in a pressure canner or they can grow botulism ([USDA canning](kiwix:usda-2015_en/home); [Botulism](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Botulism)).
- **Water** for people, animals and crops in that order; a drought-year garden is watered at the roots in the evening ([Water module](module:water)).
- **Keep animals lawful and alive.** Register any birds you keep, obey housing orders during bird flu, and know the foot-and-mouth rules on movement ([Register as a bird keeper](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/collections/register-as-a-bird-keeper); [Bird flu](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/bird-flu-avian-influenza-latest-situation-in-england)).

{{module:growing-food}}

{{module:livestock}}

{{module:water}}

## First month

- **Foraging within the law.** Picking wild fruit, foliage, fungi and flowers for your own use is not theft ([Theft Act 1968 s4](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1968/60/section/4)); uprooting without permission is an offence and protected plants may not be picked ([Wildlife and Countryside Act 1981 s13](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1981/69/section/13)). Nettles, blackberries, rosehips (the wartime vitamin C), hazelnuts, sweet chestnuts, acorns leached of their tannin, seaweed and shellfish from clean water are the calories and vitamins that grow wild in Britain ([Urtica dioica](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Urtica_dioica); [Rose hip](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rose_hip); [Acorn](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Acorn); [Edible seaweed](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Edible_seaweed)); shellfish carry poisoning and norovirus ([Paralytic shellfish poisoning](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Paralytic_shellfish_poisoning)). The rules are on [Foraging law](page:foraging-law).
- **Fishing and shooting.** A rod licence and the owner's permission in England and Wales, the close season on rivers, and the Salmon and Freshwater Fisheries Act 1975 ([Rod fishing rules](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/freshwater-rod-fishing-rules); [Salmon and Freshwater Fisheries Act 1975](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1975/51/contents)); rabbits and pigeons with the landowner's permission, deer only in season with a certificated rifle, no dogs for hunting mammals ([GOV.UK hunting](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/hunting); [Hunting Act 2004](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2004/37/contents); [Deer Act 1991](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1991/54/contents)). Rabbits, wood pigeons and grey squirrels are the abundant wild meat ([European rabbit](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/European_rabbit); [Common wood pigeon](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Common_wood_pigeon)).
- **Nutrition.** Signs of malnutrition are weight loss, tiredness, poor healing and infections; children stop growing ([Malnutrition (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/malnutrition/)). Scurvy from no fresh food in three months (bleeding gums, bruising) is cured by any vitamin C ([Scurvy (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/scurvy/)); rickets in children from no vitamin D is prevented by sun and oily fish ([Rickets (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/rickets-and-osteomalacia/)). Fat and protein run out before starch: eggs, milk, pulses, nuts and fish are the priority ([Medical module](module:medical)).
- **Community kitchens.** The wartime British Restaurants fed a hot meal for a set price to anyone; a village hall with one cooker does the same ([British Restaurant](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/British_Restaurant); [Community module](module:community)).

{{module:medical}}

{{module:community}}

## Long term

Ireland lost a million people to hunger and a million to emigration between 1845 and 1852 when blight took the one crop most people ate ([Great Famine (Ireland)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Great_Famine_(Ireland)); [Phytophthora infestans](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Phytophthora_infestans)); the Dutch Hunger Winter of 1944 to 1945 killed 18,000 in a modern country cut off from food ([Dutch famine of 1944–1945](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Dutch_famine_of_1944–1945)). The lessons are the same: many crops not one, seed saved from open-pollinated varieties, soil fed with compost, manure and nitrogen-fixing beans instead of bought fertiliser, rotation to break disease, and animals that turn what people cannot eat into eggs, milk and meat ([Growing food module](module:growing-food); [Crop rotation](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Crop_rotation); [Nitrogen fixation](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nitrogen_fixation)). Feeding a starving person is itself dangerous: small frequent meals for the first days, or refeeding syndrome can kill ([Refeeding syndrome](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Refeeding_syndrome)). The [long rebuild playbook](playbook:long-rebuild) is the decade after.

{{module:security-law}}

## UK specifics

- **The law of the land.** Foraging for your own use is lawful; selling it is not; uprooting needs permission; Schedule 8 plants are protected ([Theft Act 1968 s4](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1968/60/section/4); [Wildlife and Countryside Act 1981 s13](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1981/69/section/13)). Open access land in England and Wales and Scotland's right of responsible access let you walk, not pick crops or take game ([CRoW Act 2000 s2](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2000/37/section/2); [access land overlay](map:?overlay=access-land&overlay=footpaths)). Taking crops from a field is theft ([Theft Act 1968 s1](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1968/60/section/1)).
- **Animals.** The Animal Welfare Act duty of care applies in a famine ([Animal Welfare Act 2006](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2006/45/contents)); killing your own animal for your own table is lawful with proper stunning ([WATOK 2015](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2015/1782/contents)); pigs need a holding number ([Keeping a pet pig](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/keeping-a-pet-pig-or-micropig)).
- **Disease.** Foot and mouth closed the countryside in 2001 ([2001 United Kingdom foot-and-mouth outbreak](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2001_United_Kingdom_foot-and-mouth_outbreak)); housing orders during bird flu are law ([Bird flu](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/bird-flu-avian-influenza-latest-situation-in-england)).
- **Precedent.** Britain rationed from 1940 to 1954 and imported less than it does now; the Ministry of Food, points and the Woolton pie are the working model ([Rationing in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom); [Minister of Food](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Minister_of_Food)).
- **Water** for irrigation from rivers needs an abstraction licence above 20 cubic metres a day in England ([Water Industry Act 1991](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1991/56/contents)); rainwater and grey water are yours ([Water module](module:water)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Store counted in days at 2,000 to 2,500 kcal a person; weekly ration written {#count-calories}
- [ ] Every bed, tub and lawn edge planted; seed potatoes chitting; seed saved {#plant-everything}
- [ ] Compost heap, manure source and nitrogen-fixing beans in the rotation {#feed-the-soil}
- [ ] Hens registered; housing-order rules known; rabbits or goats considered {#animals-lawful}
- [ ] Pressure canner or drying racks ready for the glut; botulism rule learned {#preserve-safely}
- [ ] Foraging rules and the two deadly plants learned by every adult {#foraging-rules}
- [ ] Rod licence, permission and seasons checked before fishing or shooting {#fishing-and-shooting-law}
- [ ] Vitamin C and vitamin D sources for children planned: rosehips, eggs, sun {#vitamins-for-children}
- [ ] Community kitchen or shared cooker organised in the hall {#community-kitchen}
- [ ] Refeeding rule known: small meals first for anyone who has gone hungry {#refeeding-rule}

## Go deeper

- [Growing food module](module:growing-food)
- [Livestock module](module:livestock)

- [FSA safe foraging](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/guidance-for-safe-foraging/guidance-for-safe-foraging)
- [Rationing in the United Kingdom (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom)
- [Famine (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Famine)
- [Gardening Q&A](kiwix:gardening.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)

```

`playbooks/scenarios/impact-winter.md`:

```markdown
---
id: impact-winter
title: Nuclear or impact winter
icon: cloud
order: 18
summary: Years of cold, dark summers after a nuclear exchange, supervolcano or asteroid. Insulation and fuel for years, growing under cover, vitamin D, morale.
modules: [shelter-heat, growing-food, food, water, power, livestock, medical, community, mental-health]
overlays: [health, water, fuel]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: Nuclear winter (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nuclear_winter
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Impact winter (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Impact_winter
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Year Without a Summer (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Year_Without_a_Summer
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Volcanic winter of 536 (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_winter_of_536
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Nuclear War Survival Skills (Kearny, 1987)
    doc: nwss
    as_at: 1987-01
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Approved Document L volume 1
    doc: ad-l1
    as_at: 2026-03
  - title: Vitamin D (NHS)
    kiwix: nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/vitamins-and-minerals/vitamin-d/
    as_at: 2026-09
---

## Right now

**Know what it is.** Soot from burning cities after a nuclear exchange, sulphur from a very large eruption, or dust from an asteroid strike, lofted high enough to stay for years, cools the surface by several degrees and cuts the light that crops need ([Nuclear winter](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nuclear_winter); [Impact winter](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Impact_winter)). The signs are a sun that stays dim and red for weeks, frosts in the wrong months and a failed harvest. Tambora's 1815 eruption gave 1816 frosts in July and famine across Europe ([Year Without a Summer](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Year_Without_a_Summer)); the dimming of 536 brought eighteen months without proper sun, then famine and plague ([Volcanic winter of 536](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_winter_of_536)).

**The first decisions are about this year's food and next year's seed.** Harvest everything that can be harvested, store it as if it must last two years, and set aside seed before anyone eats it ([Growing food module](module:growing-food)). Whatever caused it, the immediate playbook is the one for the cause: [nuclear war](playbook:nuclear-war), [volcanic ash](playbook:volcanic) or [severe winter](playbook:severe-winter).

**Accept the argument and plan for the worse case.** The 1987 survival manual called nuclear winter a myth ([NWSS, p. 23](doc:nwss#page=23)); the climate models say a large exchange cools the world for years ([Nuclear winter](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nuclear_winter)). A household that prepared for the cold and got a mild year lost nothing.

{{module:shelter-heat}}

## First 72 hours

- **Fuel.** A winter that lasts years is heated with wood, and wood is cut a year ahead to dry; start coppice and stacks now ([Coppicing](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Coppicing); [Wood fuel](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Wood_fuel)). Coal and peat where they exist ([Peat](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Peat)). A stove with a safe flue and a carbon monoxide alarm ([Approved Document J, p. 43](doc:ad-j#page=43)).
- **Insulation.** One warm room now; then the whole house to the current standard as materials allow: loft, walls, floors, windows ([Approved Document L volume 1](doc:ad-l1); [Building insulation](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Building_insulation)).
- **Water** freezes: bury pipes, keep a trickle, store indoors, and melt snow ([Water module](module:water)).
- **Power.** Solar panels give a fraction of their output under a dimmed sky; wind and micro-hydro do not care about the light ([Micro hydro](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Micro_hydro); [Wind turbine](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Wind_turbine); [Power module](module:power)).
- **Animals** eat what people cannot: hay, tops, scraps; rabbits and hens are the efficient ones; goats give milk on rough grazing ([Livestock module](module:livestock)).
- **The register's** nearest scenarios are a nuclear war elsewhere with global economic and migration effects ([NRR 2025, p. 185](doc:nrr-2025#page=185)) and a week of deep snow ([NRR 2025, p. 143](doc:nrr-2025#page=143)); a years-long winter is beyond it.

{{module:water}}

{{module:power}}

{{module:livestock}}

## First month

- **Grow under cover.** Greenhouses, polytunnels, cold frames and cloches gain weeks of season and degrees of warmth ([Greenhouse](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Greenhouse); [Cold frame](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cold_frame)); indoors, sprouted seeds and microgreens grow in days with little light ([Sprouting](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sprouting); [Microgreen](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Microgreen)); mushrooms need no light at all ([Fungiculture](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fungiculture)).
- **Choose cold crops.** Potatoes, kale, leeks, turnips, swedes, Jerusalem artichokes, broad beans and oats tolerate cold and low light ([Potato](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Potato); [Kale](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Kale); [Rutabaga](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rutabaga); [Jerusalem artichoke](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Jerusalem_artichoke)); a root cellar keeps them ([Root cellar](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Root_cellar)).
- **Vitamin D.** Without sun everyone needs it from food or tablets: oily fish, eggs, liver; 10 micrograms a day is the NHS figure for adults in winter ([Vitamin D (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/vitamins-and-minerals/vitamin-d/)); children without it get rickets ([Rickets (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/rickets-and-osteomalacia/)).
- **Ration.** Calories per person per day, written down, with children and the sick first ([Famine playbook](playbook:famine); [Food module](module:food)).
- **Dark and mood.** Months of dim light bring seasonal depression; bright lamps when there is power, routine, work outdoors in the middle of the day, and watching each other ([Seasonal affective disorder](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Seasonal_affective_disorder); [Mental health module](module:mental-health)).

{{module:growing-food}}

{{module:food}}

{{module:medical}}

## Long term

The Little Ice Age, centuries of cold summers, was survived in Britain with hardier crops, more livestock and a great deal of hunger ([Little Ice Age](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Little_Ice_Age)); the Toba eruption 74,000 years ago may have reduced humanity to thousands ([Youngest Toba eruption](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Youngest_Toba_eruption)); the Chicxulub impact ended the dinosaurs ([Chicxulub crater](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Chicxulub_crater)). The scale decides everything, and nobody will know it in the first year. Plan in three-year units: fuel cut and drying, seed saved from what grew, a herd small enough to feed, a community that pools tunnels, tools and labour ([Community module](module:community)). The [long rebuild playbook](playbook:long-rebuild) is the rest.

{{module:community}}

{{module:mental-health}}

## UK specifics

- **Britain's advantage** is the sea: the Gulf Stream and the ocean around us damp temperature swings, and the coast gives seaweed, shellfish and fish when fields fail ([Climate of the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Climate_of_the_United_Kingdom); [Edible seaweed](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Edible_seaweed)).
- **Britain's disadvantage** is the north: a short growing season shortened further puts Scotland and the uplands first into famine, as in the 1690s ([Little Ice Age](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Little_Ice_Age)).
- **Housing stock** is old and leaky; the insulation standard for new homes shows what an existing one should aim at ([Approved Document L volume 1](doc:ad-l1)); stoves and flues have rules ([Approved Document J, p. 11](doc:ad-j#page=11)).
- **Gas and oil** run out or stop being delivered before wood does; the register's gas scenario runs October to May ([NRR 2025, p. 62](doc:nrr-2025#page=62)).
- **Cold-Health Alerts** and Cold Weather Payments exist while the state does ([AWHP, p. 34](doc:awhp-2026#page=34); [Cold Weather Payment](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/cold-weather-payment)); the hospitals, water works and fuel stations are on the map for as long as they run ([overlay](map:?overlay=health&overlay=water&overlay=fuel)).

## Checklist

- [ ] This year's harvest stored for two years; seed set aside before anyone eats {#store-and-seed}
- [ ] Wood cut and stacked to dry a year ahead; coppice planted {#wood-a-year-ahead}
- [ ] Stove, flue and carbon monoxide alarm sound; a season's fuel by the door {#stove-ready}
- [ ] Loft, walls, floors and windows insulated as far as materials allow {#insulate-the-house}
- [ ] Greenhouse, tunnel or cold frames built; sprouting jars and mushroom beds indoors {#grow-under-cover}
- [ ] Cold crops chosen: potatoes, kale, leeks, roots, broad beans, oats {#cold-crops}
- [ ] Vitamin D for every child and adult: fish, eggs, liver, tablets while they last {#vitamin-d}
- [ ] Rabbits, hens and a goat on food people cannot eat; hay stored {#efficient-animals}
- [ ] Pipes buried or lagged; indoor water store; snow-melting routine {#water-in-cold}
- [ ] Wind or hydro power explored; solar expectations lowered {#power-without-sun}
- [ ] Daily routine, midday light and a watch on everyone's mood {#mood-and-routine}

## Go deeper

- [Shelter and heat module](module:shelter-heat)
- [Growing food module](module:growing-food)
- [Nuclear winter (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nuclear_winter)
- [Impact event (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Impact_event)
- [Volcanic winter (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Volcanic_winter)
- [Global dimming (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Global_dimming)
- [Hydroponics (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hydroponics)
- [Gardening Q&A](kiwix:gardening.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)
- [Approved Document L, insulation](doc:ad-l1)
- [NWSS on nuclear winter](doc:nwss#page=23)
- [Famine playbook](playbook:famine)
- [Long rebuild playbook](playbook:long-rebuild)
```

`playbooks/scenarios/terrorism.md`:

```markdown
---
id: terrorism
title: Mass-casualty terrorism
icon: alert
order: 19
summary: A bombing, a marauding knife or vehicle attack, or a poisoning. Run, hide, tell; stop the bleeding; avoid the second device; the delay and grief after.
modules: [medical, comms, evacuation, mental-health, security-law, community]
overlays: [health, rail]
reviewed: null
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Run, hide, tell (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Run,_hide,_tell
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Manchester Arena bombing (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Manchester_Arena_bombing
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: 7 July 2005 London bombings (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/7_July_2005_London_bombings
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Terrorism (Protection of Premises) Act 2025 (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Terrorism_(Protection_of_Premises)_Act_2025
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: FM 4-25.11 First Aid
    doc: fm-4-25-11-first-aid
    as_at: 2002-12
  - title: Emergency War Surgery
    doc: emergency-war-surgery-2018
    as_at: 2018-01
  - title: Coping with trauma (Prepare)
    kiwix: prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/coping-with-trauma/
    as_at: 2026-09
---

## Right now

**Run, hide, tell.** Run if there is a route away from the danger, leaving belongings; hide if you cannot, out of sight, phone silent, door barricaded; tell the police on 999 when you are safe, with what you saw, where and when ([Run, hide, tell](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Run,_hide,_tell)). Do not stop to film; do not gather to watch; do not go back for anything.

**Second device.** After an explosion, move well away from the scene and out of sight of it, not towards it; bombers plan for the crowd that gathers and for the responders ([Improvised explosive device](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Improvised_explosive_device)). The register's scenario is a device in a crowded place with further deaths from collapse, fire and crush ([NRR 2025, p. 31](doc:nrr-2025#page=31)).

**Stop the bleeding.** Once you are out of the danger, the first-aid that saves lives in the minutes before ambulances arrive is pressure, packing and a tourniquet on a limb, and keeping the person warm ([Severe bleeding card](card:severe-bleeding); [FM 4-25.11, p. 41](doc:fm-4-25-11-first-aid#page=41)). Armed police and specialist teams enter first; ambulances wait until the scene is declared safe, which can be a long time ([NRR 2025, p. 32](doc:nrr-2025#page=32)).

**Poison or gas** in a crowd: away, upwind, clothes off, wash; tell 999 what you smelt or saw ([Chemical exposure card](card:chemical-exposure); [Chemical playbook](playbook:chemical)).

{{module:medical}}

## First 72 hours

- **What the state expects.** Marauding attacks with vehicles, knives or firearms and bombings in venues and public spaces are the terrorism risks scored most likely in the register (a likelihood of more than 25%), with the 2017 Manchester Arena bombing (22 killed), the 2017 London Bridge attack (8) and the 2020 Reading attack (3) as its examples ([NRR 2025, p. 31](doc:nrr-2025#page=31); [NRR 2025, p. 32](doc:nrr-2025#page=32)).
- **Casualties.** Blast injuries include burst eardrums, lung injury that shows hours later, and shrapnel wounds; anyone near an explosion who seems fine still needs to be seen ([Blast injury](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Blast_injury); [Hearing loss (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/hearing-loss/)). Crush injuries release toxins when the weight comes off ([Crush injury](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Crush_injury)). Triage rules when there are more casualties than hands are in the field manual ([Emergency War Surgery, p. 38](doc:emergency-war-surgery-2018#page=38)).
- **Finding people.** The police open a casualty bureau; hospitals cannot tell you who they have; keep one phone free and one person at home by it ([Communications module](module:comms)).
- **Transport.** Stations and the area close for days ([stations overlay](map:?overlay=rail&overlay=health)); the attack "may temporarily impact utility supply, transport services and put pressure on emergency services" ([NRR 2025, p. 31](doc:nrr-2025#page=31)).
- **Rumour.** Names, numbers and "second attack" claims spread within minutes and are mostly wrong; the SHARE checklist before forwarding ([NRR 2025, p. 22](doc:nrr-2025#page=22)).
- **Displacement.** Homes near the scene are evacuated, sometimes for days while the building is made safe ([NRR 2025, p. 31](doc:nrr-2025#page=31); [Evacuation module](module:evacuation)).

{{module:comms}}

{{module:evacuation}}

## First month

- **Hospitals.** "The short-term excessive demands on hospitals may lead to delays in the system for several weeks" ([NRR 2025, p. 32](doc:nrr-2025#page=32)); expect cancelled appointments and long A&E waits across the region; use 111 and the cards for everything that is not life-threatening ([Medical module](module:medical)).
- **Victim support** is organised locally, regionally and nationally; take it, for the practical help as much as the counselling ([NRR 2025, p. 31](doc:nrr-2025#page=31)).
- **Trauma.** Nightmares, jumpiness and avoidance in the first weeks are normal; if they are no better after four weeks it may be PTSD and it is treatable ([Coping with trauma](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/coping-with-trauma/); [PTSD (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/mental-health/conditions/ptsd-post-traumatic-stress-disorder/)). Children need routine, honest simple answers and less news ([Mental health module](module:mental-health)).
- **Bereavement.** Identification can take days; the coroner, the casualty bureau and the family liaison officer are the contacts; grief has no timetable ([Grief (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/mental-health/feelings-symptoms-behaviours/feelings-and-symptoms/grief-bereavement-loss/)).
- **Community tension** follows some attacks; the register's own advice is to keep politics and blame out of the response ([NRR 2025, p. 23](doc:nrr-2025#page=23); [Community module](module:community)).

{{module:mental-health}}

## Long term

The 7 July 2005 bombings killed 52 people on three trains and a bus; the inquests, the memorials and the survivors' injuries lasted decades ([7 July 2005 London bombings](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/7_July_2005_London_bombings)). Manchester's inquiry produced Martyn's Law, which requires venues and events to plan for an attack ([Manchester Arena bombing](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Manchester_Arena_bombing); [Terrorism (Protection of Premises) Act 2025](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Terrorism_(Protection_of_Premises)_Act_2025)). A chemical, biological or radiological attack, though judged less likely than knives, vehicles and bombs, could close areas for years ([NRR 2025, p. 40](doc:nrr-2025#page=40)); the [chemical](playbook:chemical) and [nuclear accident](playbook:nuclear-accident) playbooks cover the response.

{{module:security-law}}

{{module:community}}

## UK specifics

- **Numbers.** 999 for the attack; the anti-terrorist hotline and the casualty bureau numbers are announced on the day; 111 for everything else; Samaritans 116 123 ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)).
- **Salisbury 2018** is the UK's chemical-attack precedent: a nerve agent on a door handle, two survivors, one death from a discarded bottle months later ([Poisoning of Sergei and Yulia Skripal](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Poisoning_of_Sergei_and_Yulia_Skripal); [NRR 2025, p. 40](doc:nrr-2025#page=40)); Litvinenko in 2006 is the radiological one.
- **Martyn's Law** (the Terrorism (Protection of Premises) Act 2025) makes the people responsible for venues and events plan and train; the village hall and the church may be within it ([Terrorism (Protection of Premises) Act 2025](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Terrorism_(Protection_of_Premises)_Act_2025)).
- **The law on you.** Carrying anything as a weapon in case of attack is an offence ([Knife and firearms law](page:knife-firearms-law)); possessing material useful to terrorism is an offence under the Terrorism Act 2000 s58 ([Terrorism Act 2000 s58](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2000/11/section/58)); reasonable force to stop an attacker is lawful ([CJIA 2008 s76](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2008/4/section/76)).
- **Northern Ireland-related terrorism** remains a separate entry in the register ([NRR 2025, p. 29](doc:nrr-2025#page=29)); the response advice is the same.
- **Who responds:** armed police, Hazardous Area Response Teams and fire service specialist teams first, under the Joint Emergency Services Interoperability Principles, with Local Resilience Forums managing the consequences ([NRR 2025, p. 32](doc:nrr-2025#page=32)).

## Checklist

- [ ] Run, hide, tell taught to every member of the household, children included {#run-hide-tell}
- [ ] Exits noted on arrival at any crowded place; meeting point agreed outside {#exits-and-meeting-point}
- [ ] Bleeding kit carried in the bag: two dressings, packing gauze, a tourniquet, gloves {#carry-bleeding-kit}
- [ ] After an explosion: away and out of sight; no gathering, no filming {#second-device-rule}
- [ ] One phone kept free; one person at home by it {#phone-free-at-home}
- [ ] Anyone near a blast seen by a doctor even if they feel fine {#blast-check}
- [ ] SHARE checklist before forwarding any name, number or rumour {#share-before-forward}
- [ ] Non-urgent care routed to 111 and the cards for the weeks of hospital delay {#route-around-hospitals}
- [ ] Four-week rule for trauma symptoms known; help sought if they persist {#four-week-rule}
- [ ] Children's routine kept; news limited {#children-routine}

## Go deeper

- [Severe bleeding card](card:severe-bleeding)
- [Medical module](module:medical)
- [Shock card](card:shock)
- [Burns card](card:burns)
- [Run, hide, tell (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Run,_hide,_tell)
- [Emergency tourniquet (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Emergency_tourniquet)
- [Triage (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Triage)
- [Emergency War Surgery, triage](doc:emergency-war-surgery-2018#page=38)
- [Ship Captain's Medical Guide, care of the injured](doc:scmg-ch04)
- [Attacks in public places (Ready.gov)](kiwix:www.ready.gov_en/www.ready.gov/attacks-public-places)

- [Chemical playbook](playbook:chemical)
```
