    as_at: 2026-02-15
---

## Right now

**Survive the first month with the playbook for the cause**, then come back here. The register's own words on recovery: it "can last months, years or even decades" and works best when the affected community takes part in deciding how ([NRR 2025, p. 22](doc:nrr-2025#page=22)). Every household action it recommends for the first days still applies: water, food, warmth, a radio, neighbours ([NRR 2025, p. 21](doc:nrr-2025#page=21)).

**Order of work:** water, then sanitation, then food, then shelter, then security, then everything else ([Water module](module:water)). Cholera and typhoid, not violence, emptied cities that lost their sewers ([Cholera (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/cholera/); [Sanitation module](module:sanitation)).

**Take stock of people.** Skills, tools, animals, land, seed, medicines, fuel, and who needs care: the community plan's asset and skills registers, on paper, in the hall ([Community module](module:community)).

{{module:community}}

{{module:water}}

{{module:sanitation}}

## First 72 hours

- **Water for the street.** A well, a spring or a stream with a settling tank, a cloth filter and chlorine or boiling; a hand pump on a borehole is the long-term answer ([Well](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Well); [Hand pump](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hand_pump); [Water Treatment Library](kiwix:zimgit-water_en/home)).
- **Latrines** 30 metres from any water and downhill of it, or composting toilets; the same rules for burying the dead ([Pit latrine](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Pit_latrine); [Approved Document H, p. 31](doc:ad-h#page=31); [After a death](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/after-a-death)).
- **Food.** Ration what is stored, plant everything, and organise the animals ([Food module](module:food); [Famine playbook](playbook:famine)).
- **Security without weapons:** locks, a dog, a watch rota, agreements with neighbours, no displays of plenty; the law of theft and reasonable force does not lapse because the police cannot come ([Security and the law module](module:security-law)).
- **Records.** A ledger of who has what, who owes what, who did what; disputes in year three are settled by the book written in week one ([Community module](module:community)).

{{module:food}}

{{module:security-law}}

{{module:shelter-heat}}

## First month

- **Medicine without the NHS.** The village handbook is Where There Is No Doctor ([Where There Is No Doctor](doc:where-there-is-no-doctor)); austere medicine, suturing, dental extraction and childbirth are in Survival and Austere Medicine ([Survival and Austere Medicine, p. 10](doc:survival-austere-medicine-2017#page=10)); the WHO essential list is the pharmacy to build ([WHO EML, p. 12](doc:who-eml-2025#page=12)). Antibiotics expire slowly; tetanus, typhoid and childbirth are the killers to plan for ([Tetanus (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/tetanus/); [Typhoid fever (NHS)](kiwix:nhs_uk/www.nhs.uk/conditions/typhoid-fever/); [Childbirth card](card:childbirth)).
- **Power.** Salvaged panels, a wind turbine, a water wheel or micro-hydro on a stream, lead-acid or lithium batteries kept above half, and 12 V everything ([Micro hydro](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Micro_hydro); [Lead–acid battery](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Lead–acid_battery); [Power module](module:power)). This box on a panel and a battery is the library.
- **Tools and repair.** Hand tools, the spares list, the knowledge to fix rather than replace ([Tools and repair module](module:tools-repair); [iFixit](kiwix:ifixit_en_all/home/home)); the Home Improvement and Woodworking Q&A for the questions that arise ([Home Improvement Q&A](kiwix:diy.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions); [Woodworking Q&A](kiwix:woodworking.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)).
- **Fuel.** Coppice woodland cut on rotation gives firewood for ever; charcoal for the forge ([Coppicing](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Coppicing); [Charcoal](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Charcoal)).
- **Navigation and range.** Footpaths, byways and the old roads; bicycles and carts; a grid reference for every place that matters ([Navigation module](module:navigation); [footpaths overlay](map:?overlay=footpaths&overlay=access-land)).

{{module:medical}}

{{module:power}}

{{module:tools-repair}}

{{module:navigation}}

## Long term

**Year one to three: food and health.** Rotation and green manure keep soil fertile without bought fertiliser ([Norfolk four-course system](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Norfolk_four-course_system); [Green manure](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Green_manure)); preserve by drying, salting, smoking and fermenting ([Food preservation](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Food_preservation); [Food drying](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Food_drying); [Sauerkraut](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sauerkraut)); bake, brew and make cheese ([Sourdough](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sourdough); [Cheesemaking](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cheesemaking); [Homebrewing Q&A](kiwix:homebrew.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)); keep bees ([Beekeeping](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Beekeeping)). Midwifery, vaccination if any vaccine survives, and clean water keep children alive ([Midwifery](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Midwifery); [Vaccination](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Vaccination)).

**Year three to ten: trades and materials.** Soap from lye and fat ([Soap](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Soap); [Lye](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Lye)); candles from tallow and beeswax ([Candle](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Candle)); lime burned for mortar and plaster ([Lime kiln](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Lime_kiln); [Lime mortar](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Lime_mortar)); timber framing, cob, thatch and wattle for buildings that stand ([Timber framing](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Timber_framing); [Cob (material)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cob_(material)); [Thatching](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Thatching); [Approved Document A](doc:ad-a)); iron from the bloomery and the forge ([Bloomery](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Bloomery); [Blacksmith](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Blacksmith)); leather, wool and cloth ([Tanning (leather)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Tanning_(leather)); [Spinning wheel](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Spinning_wheel); [Loom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Loom)); paper and salt ([Papermaking](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Papermaking); [Sea salt](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sea_salt)); mills on water and wind ([Watermill](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Watermill); [Windmill](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Windmill)); horses and carts, and the canals ([Draft horse](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Draft_horse); [Canals of the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Canals_of_the_United_Kingdom)). The appropriate-technology wiki and the engineering Q&A carry the detail ([Appropedia](kiwix:appropedia_en_all/Welcome_to_Appropedia); [Engineering Q&A](kiwix:engineering.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)); the book this playbook is named after lists what to rebuild first ([The Knowledge](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/The_Knowledge:_How_to_Rebuild_Our_World_from_Scratch)).

**Law and community.** Britain after Rome lost coinage and pottery within a generation ([Fall of the Western Roman Empire](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fall_of_the_Western_Roman_Empire)); the Black Death killed a third to a half and the survivors renegotiated everything ([Black Death in England](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Black_Death_in_England)); disease without the NHS is the [pandemic playbook](playbook:pandemic). Collapses are usually slow and partial, and what carries people through is the parish: a council that meets, a ledger, a common law of contract and theft that everyone still recognises, and mutual aid ([Societal collapse](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Societal_collapse); [Parish council (England)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Parish_council_(England)); [Common law](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Common_law); [Mutual aid](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Mutual_aid)). Barter and commodity money return before paper does ([Commodity money](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Commodity_money)).

{{module:growing-food}}

{{module:livestock}}

{{module:mental-health}}

## UK specifics

- **What Britain has.** Rain, grass, coppice woodland, coal and peat, tidal and running water, a long coast, the most detailed maps in the world and a dense network of footpaths and old roads ([footpaths overlay](map:?overlay=footpaths&overlay=access-land&overlay=rail); [Navigation module](module:navigation)). Reservoirs and water works are marked ([water overlay](map:?overlay=water)).
- **What Britain lacks.** A short growing season in the north, thin soils on the uplands, a population far above what its farmland fed before the Haber process ([Haber process](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Haber_process)); food is the constraint that decides how many people a valley holds ([Famine playbook](playbook:famine)).
- **Law that remains.** The Civil Contingencies Act's emergency regulations while any government functions ([Civil Contingencies Act 2004](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2004/36/contents)); the offences of theft, assault and criminal damage; reasonable force ([CJIA 2008 s76](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2008/4/section/76)); the weapons law that keeps a village from arming itself against itself ([Knife and firearms law](page:knife-firearms-law)).
- **The parish** is the institution with the longest record: councils in England, community councils in Scotland and Wales, with the hall, the noticeboard and the register of who needs help ([Parish council (England)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Parish_council_(England)); [Community council](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Community_council); [Ready Scotland community planning](kiwix:govuk_resilience/ready.campaign.gov.scot/community-emergency-planning)).
- **Building** to the Approved Documents where you can: structure, fire, drainage, stoves, insulation, electrics ([Approved Document A](doc:ad-a); [Approved Document B volume 1](doc:ad-b1); [Approved Document H](doc:ad-h); [Approved Document J](doc:ad-j); [Approved Document L volume 1](doc:ad-l1); [Approved Document P](doc:ad-p)).
- **This box** on a panel, a battery and a spare drive in a tin is the library the village rebuilds from; keep a second copy somewhere dry ([About Operation SOS](page:about-sos)).

### Task 8: Cross-link pass

**Files:**
- Modify: `api/tests/test_playbooks_content.py` (append)
- Modify: `tools/zimit/legislation_uk.txt` (one line), `playbooks/scenarios/terrorism.md` (one link), `playbooks/modules/comms.md` (one citation), `playbooks/modules/radiation.md` (two citations), `playbooks/cards/cpr-adult.md`, `playbooks/cards/cpr-child.md`, `playbooks/cards/recovery-position.md`, `playbooks/cards/drowning.md`, `playbooks/cards/asthma-attack.md` (one Source link each), plus any scenario the new tests name

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the complete tree from Tasks 3 to 7; `validate_tree(..., require_all_scenarios=True)`; the `SCENARIOS`, `MODULES` and `overlay_ids()` names in the test file.
- Produces: a tree in which every module is used by at least two scenarios, every overlay by at least one, every scenario links to and is linked from another, every Go deeper has at least five resolving links, every citation page found wrong during planning is corrected, and `sos validate-playbooks --all-scenarios` prints `OK 72 documents`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Append the cross-link tests**

```python
def all_scenarios() -> dict[str, frontmatter.Post]:
    return {s: load("scenarios", s) for s in SCENARIOS}


PLAYBOOK_LINK = re.compile(r"\]\(playbook:([a-z0-9-]+)\)")


def test_every_scenario_present_and_tree_validates():
    assert sorted(p.stem for p in (PB / "scenarios").glob("*.md")) == sorted(SCENARIOS)
    assert validate_tree(PB, load_manifests(MANIFEST_DIR), overlay_ids(), require_all_scenarios=True) == []


def test_every_module_used_by_at_least_two_scenarios():
    use = {m: [s for s, p in all_scenarios().items() if m in p["modules"]] for m in MODULES}
    thin = {m: s for m, s in use.items() if len(s) < 2}
    assert thin == {}, thin


def test_every_overlay_used_by_a_scenario():
    used = {o for p in all_scenarios().values() for o in p["overlays"]}
    assert set(overlay_ids()) <= used, set(overlay_ids()) - used


def test_scenario_cross_links():
    inbound = {s: 0 for s in SCENARIOS}
    for slug, post in all_scenarios().items():
        targets = set(PLAYBOOK_LINK.findall(post.content))
        assert targets, (slug, "no playbook: links")
        assert targets <= set(SCENARIOS), (slug, targets - set(SCENARIOS))
        assert slug not in targets, (slug, "links to itself")
        for t in targets:
            inbound[t] += 1
    assert [s for s, n in inbound.items() if n == 0] == []
```

Expected: `20 passed` (the per-scenario section-citation test, which is the milestone's "at least one citation in Right now, First 72 hours and UK specifics").

- [ ] **Step 4: Spot-check real article paths against the sample library on the PC**

The manifest ids are not the sample files' book names (`sos sync` renames on the box; on the PC the books are named by file stem), so this is a manual check of the path conventions, not a run of `--deep`.

```bash
kiwix-serve --address all --port 8090 --urlRootLocation /kiwix --nosearchbar --nolibrarybutton --blockexternal \
  ~/sos-content/zim/nhs.uk_en_medicines_2025-12.zim ~/sos-content/zim/zimgit-water_en_2024-08.zim \
  ~/sos-content/zim/ifixit_en_all_2025-12.zim ~/sos-content/zim/wikipedia_en_100_mini_2026-01.zim &
sleep 2
for p in nhs.uk_en_medicines_2025-12/content/www.nhs.uk/medicines/doxycycline/ \
         nhs.uk_en_medicines_2025-12/content/www.nhs.uk/medicines/insulin/ \
         nhs.uk_en_medicines_2025-12/content/www.nhs.uk/medicines/salbutamol-inhaler/ \
         zimgit-water_en_2024-08/content/home \
         ifixit_en_all_2025-12/content/home/home \
         wikipedia_en_100_mini_2026-01/content/index; do
  printf '%s  %s\n' "$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' "http://127.0.0.1:8090/kiwix/raw/$p")" "$p"
done
kill %1
```

Expected: six lines, each starting `200`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Deep check on the box (milestone 5, recorded in `docs/hardware-checklist.md`)**

After `sos sync --tier core` and `sos build-nhs` have run on the Pi:

Run: `sos validate-playbooks --deep --all-scenarios`
Expected: `OK 72 documents`. A line `scenarios/<slug>.md: kiwix:<id>/<path> returned non-200` means the article moved: open `/read/<id>/` on the box, search the title, and correct the path in every file that uses it (`grep -rl "<path>" playbooks`), then re-run. A line `doc '<id>' file missing` means `sos sync` has not fetched that item; run `sos sync --tier core --only <id>`.

- [ ] **Step 6: Reviewer sign-off, one scenario at a time**

For each of the twenty scenarios, in `order`:

1. Open `/s/<slug>` on the kiosk and on a phone; open the print view on the phone (spec milestone 4's box test) and confirm every section, module accordion and checklist item renders and the Go deeper links open.
2. Work through the scenario's line in the Task 6 or Task 7 fact-check checklist: open every citation from the text; where the source disagrees, change the text and commit `content(<slug>): correct <what>`.
3. Set the sign-off date in the front matter (unquoted, like every other date in the tree):

   ```yaml
   reviewed: 2026-09-10
   ```

4. Run: `cd api && pytest tests/test_playbooks_content.py -q -k "<slug>"`
   Expected: `3 passed` (for `nuclear-war` use `-k "nuclear-war and not accident"`; the `reviewed` assertion accepts a `YYYY-MM-DD` date as well as `null`).
5. Run: `SOS_PLAYBOOKS_DIR=playbooks SOS_MANIFEST_DIR=manifest api/.venv/bin/sos validate-playbooks --all-scenarios`
   Expected: `OK 72 documents`.
6. Commit: `git commit -am "content(<slug>): owner sign-off"`.

- [ ] **Step 7: Confirm milestone 4 is closed**

Run: `grep -L '^reviewed: 20' playbooks/scenarios/*.md`
Expected: no output (every scenario carries a `reviewed:` date). While any file is listed, the milestone is open.

Run: `grep -h '^reviewed:' playbooks/scenarios/*.md | sort | uniq -c`
Expected: one or more lines whose dates are all `YYYY-MM-DD`, summing to 20.

Run: `make test 2>&1 | tail -n 1`
Expected: `OK 72 documents`.

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**

```bash
git add Makefile playbooks/scenarios
git commit -m "content: milestone 4 closed; all twenty scenarios reviewed"
```

**Fact-check checklist (reviewer, Task 9):**
- [ ] `docs/hardware-checklist.md` has a dated, committed line for the `--deep` run on the box and for the phone print view of one playbook.
- [ ] Every `reviewed:` date is on or after the date of the commit that last changed that scenario's body (`git log -1 --format=%cs -- playbooks/scenarios/<slug>.md`).

## Decisions for other plans

Recorded here because the deviation list at the top promised it and because each was found while writing this plan.

1. **Plan 01, `sync.py` (OPDS resolution).** `?name=<bare name>` returns every flavour. For an id ending `_maxi`, `_nopic` or `_mini`, pick the catalogue entry whose `<flavour>` equals that token; for any other id pick the newest entry. `nhs_medicines` is the one id whose `source.name` (`nhs.uk_en_medicines`) is not derivable from the id; the resolver reads `source.name`, never the id.
2. **Plan 01, `content.py` (`parse_document`).** Every date in this plan's front matter is unquoted (`as_at: 2025-01-16`, `reviewed: 2026-09-10`), so PyYAML delivers `datetime.date` objects. Convert them with `isoformat()` before JSON-schema validation and before returning `Document`, so the schema's string patterns and the `Playbook.reviewed: string | null` contract both hold. `as_at: 2026-09` stays a string.
3. **Plan 01, `content.py` (`_links`).** Link targets contain balanced parentheses (`kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Katla_(volcano)`, `Faster_Payment_System_(United_Kingdom)`, `Parish_council_(England)`); the link regex must not stop at the first `)`. markdown-it-py already handles these when rendering; the validator and `resolve_link` must match it.
4. **Plan 01, `build-nhs`.** nhs.uk now redirects its first-aid pages to sja.org.uk; the crawler must stay on `www.nhs.uk` (no off-site redirects) and `--deep` must treat a redirect to another host as a failure, not a 200. The crawl scope must include `/symptoms/` (used for `diarrhoea-and-vomiting` and `toothache`) as spec section 13 lists.
5. **Plan 01, Makefile.** `make test` runs `validate-playbooks --all-scenarios` from Task 9 onward.
6. **Plan 01, `library.py` / `routers/map.py` (`scenarios_on`).** Derived from scenario `overlays:` at index time. With this plan's front matter the mapping is: `footpaths` → invasion, famine, long-rebuild; `access-land` → famine, long-rebuild; `flood-zones` → storms-flooding, heat-drought; `health` → every scenario except invasion, famine; `fuel` → grid-collapse, solar-storm, emp, cyber-attack, invasion, civil-unrest, economic-collapse, supply-chain, severe-winter, impact-winter; `water` → nuclear-war, nuclear-accident, grid-collapse, cyber-attack, heat-drought, chemical, famine, impact-winter, long-rebuild; `rail` → grid-collapse, emp, invasion, civil-unrest, supply-chain, storms-flooding, severe-winter, terrorism, long-rebuild; `nuclear-sites` → nuclear-war, nuclear-accident; `chemical-sites` → chemical; `airports-military` → nuclear-war, invasion, volcanic. Plan 02's map tests can use these as fixtures.
7. **Plan 02, `icons.tsx`.** Add glyphs for the names this plan's front matter uses that the icon set lacks: modules `water food toilet compass people exit car tools seedling cow`; cards `child blood lungs bone baby bandage brain syringe face bed sugar`; pages `antenna plug clipboard leaf bug info`; scenarios `atom zap laptop flag waves mountain cloud`. Until then they draw the `help` glyph; nothing else breaks.
8. **Plan 04, `build-maps`.** Reads `tools/map-styles/data/nuclear-sites.geojson` (Task 2). The hand-authored part of `chemical-sites` should include at least Grangemouth, Stanlow, Fawley, the Humber refineries, the Teesside, Runcorn and Ellesmere Port chemical complexes and the Buncefield-type fuel terminals, because the chemical scenario names them.
9. **Plan 05, `tools/eval/questions.jsonl`.** The twenty scenarios and the facts in their fact-check checklists are the natural `answer` cases (expected item and path are the citation targets); the 23 card titles are the `health` cases for the router.
10. **PC development.** In `~/sos-content/zim` the kiwix book names are file stems (`nhs.uk_en_medicines_2025-12`), not manifest ids; only `sos sync` on the box renames files to `zim/<id>.zim`. Dev fixtures and tests must not assume the two are equal.
